the power wires are drawn for one extra draw call; and the craft menu's icon-less rows went from nine to two, both of which now say WHY. **THE PLAYTEST PASS (GP-53 to GP-56), 2026-07-27:** the sinking is fixed at its root and it was NOT the hypothesis it was briefed as. `StructureBodies.deckUnder` marched down from the feet and clamped its answer to `min(rFrom, r + 0.05)`, which made the floor a function of WHERE THE FEET WERE rather than a property of the structure, so the ground snap ratified each tick's gravity penetration instead of correcting it. Measured on a stationary, grounded, 0.00 m/s player standing on a deck he had walked onto: **0.198989 m of standing spread over 300 ticks, 4 snap-ups at a mean period of 74.3 ticks, sinking 0.002683 m every tick, up to 0.149774 m below the deck's own top face; after, 0.000000 on all four**. The terrain won the `deck > groundR` comparison on **0 of 300 ticks**, so the briefed hypothesis is refuted by measurement. The same clamp had a second victim nobody had reported: a foundation laid on the cell the player stands in puts its whole 0.50 m slab ABOVE the feet, a downward-only march never saw it, and the player stood **0.499993 m below their own first foundation with the port answering nothing on 120 of 120 ticks**. It is now an exact slab intersection of the radial with each box in its own frame, with a rise allowance of one published step so a floor you are standing INSIDE seats you on top of it and a 4 m wall never can. And the space half of the game got an entrance: the assembly bay's launch key was swallowed by a GLOBAL `UI_ALLOWED` and then discarded again by an explicit gate, so a player who built a rocket and pressed the launch key at it got NOTHING; `Input.setUiCapture` now takes a per-panel allowance, the bay has a visible **Roll out G** button, the checklist names the bay and the roll-out from the first minute, and right-click in the bay finally reaches `removeAt`, which had shipped complete with refunds and had no caller but the debug surface. **THE LAUNCH PAD IS A REAL BUILDING (GP-57 to GP-60), 2026-07-27:** the shipped 24 x 24 m pad is placeable, gated behind a new `Launch Facilities` tech, saved, and rockets roll out onto its published `socket_vessel` at a MEASURED 1.84e-10 m, with the pad's four clamps swinging back on the SAME fixed tick the launch clamp releases (938 against 938, stamped by two systems) and a negative control at TWR 0.978 where nothing moves. It stands on a 6 x 6 platform of foundations and NEVER on soil, because a 24 m footprint is accepted on **3.7%** of 81 sampled origins against **59.3%** for a 4 m one on the same terrain in the same run. And Reid's "when i click to place a foundation, it typically places multiple" is fixed at the shared seam after two rejected fixes that both looked like they worked. ** · **THE FACTORIO MACHINE INTERACTION MODEL (GP-61 to GP-64), 2026-07-27:** left-clicking a machine with a bare hand opens its screen, and ONE screen serves both machine families (the hand furnace behind a WASM handle and a factory smelter behind a build index), with input, fuel and output as CLICKABLE SLOTS. The progress bar needed **no new export and no ABI bump** because both counters already existed and neither was ever on screen, and it is measured AT THE PIXEL: `barPct` is read back off the element's own width, so the assertion is the painted bar against the sim rather than the client against a second copy of its own arithmetic, and it comes out **worst |drawn - sim| = 0 points over 181 samples**, 3 sweeps for 3 units, peak 99%, with the sim spending the authored budget exactly (526 ticks against 180 x 3 less the 14 already made) and the factory smelter proven separately over `of_net_progress01` at peak 97% and worst error 0. **100% is never sampled and cannot be**, because `Furnace::tick` completes a unit and zeroes `progress_` in the same tick, so a literal-100 assertion could only be satisfied by a client that faked it. And the roaming toast is gone from routine production only: the flash path was CHECKED first and carries four things (boot progress, the felled moment, an objective tick, the ingot), the ingot was the only routine one, the cue moved to the machine rather than being deleted (`ingotCues` still rises once per unit, which is what stops a build that had merely gone deaf from passing), and the ledger across the WHOLE session reads **1 banner, 1 objective, 3 ingots**. The panel stopped hiding itself in the cockpit, which it had been doing on the same frame the row became possible. **AND THE PLANNER SAYS HOW LONG THE TRIP IS, WHICH IT NEVER HAD**: the moon reads **`trip takes 10:34:49`** at its cheapest departure against the executor's `light it in 2:16:59`, both drawn, asserted a minute apart so the screen cannot be saying one thing twice; the number was in `of_ap_departure_curve` word 3 all along and the readout dropped it. `playthrough.js`'s `timed out` on the moon leg decomposed: **its 90 s box bought 8177 s of sim against a first ignition 8219 s out**, so it was 42 seconds short and had never hung. **AND THE TWO CHARTS STOPPED LOOKING IDENTICAL WHILE BEHAVING DIFFERENTLY**: the station solves 64 of 64 and the moon 38 of 64, and both answers are right, so the choice made was to make them LOOK different, not behave the same. Refusals are drawn as shaded columns (**6 rects against 6 polyline runs**), the counts are stated on both (`64 of 64 priced / 0 refused` against `38 of 64 priced / 26 refused`), and the affordability rule is always on the chart, pinned to an edge when it is off-scale, **which is the state it used to vanish in: every departure priced and none affordable**, reproduced with a pod-and-engine vehicle reading `aff below`, verdict `never`, arm disabled. Four negative controls, all red by name, all reverted byte-identical. And `probes/mapplanner.js` had been **red at HEAD** since GP-291/GP-295 retired the premise it was asserting, verified against HEAD's own sources before being touched · · **THE MOON IS IN THE MENU (GP-500 to GP-503), 2026-08-03:** an **Another world** group in the cheat panel with one row per body, the body you are standing on **disabled and refusing by name**, and the trip taken as a **page reload into `?body=cinder`** rather than through `of.reboot(1)`, chosen on three measurements, the deciding one being that a rebooted session saves the moon's world under body 0 and eats the player's Forge world (persistence R-BODY-2). The decisive argument is not the hint: **`DigAction.step` takes the SAME `use` frame and its own comment says the cooldown makes it repeat, so one button repeated at dirt and did not repeat at a tree.** After, one hold fells it: **5 swings, 25 Wood, `tree felled` on screen**; the control restores the edge latch alone and reproduces `swings: 1, granted: 5` exactly, byte-identical revert, with `impact`, `harvest`, `objectives`, `goalhints` and `cheats` all green. **Four checklist hints read as broken English in the DEFAULT hotbar layout** (`craft a furnace, 2 and Left click place it, E opens it`) because GP-165 derived every key and never gave the derivation a grammar; the phrase moved into the function that knows which branch it took. **Two player-facing reasons cited decision IDs** (`(DW-31 / GP-82)`, `(GP-101)`) and **the Peaceful mode chip read `OFF` in the one mode that is safe by construction**, i.e. the opposite of the world. **REPORTED AND DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED, because each is a design call or a cross-lane blast radius:** sandbox is not applied consistently to the three progression screens and they contradict each other in one session (build menu `free (sandbox)` beside `Stone 0/40`, craft rows red `Iron 0/5` beside a live Craft button, research `0 / 7 unlocked` gating everything while the checklist tells you to research a pad the build menu is already giving away); Credits nothing to pollution/evolution by construction (never calls into /core's wave loop at all). Takes a plain position + seed today; the POI bridge lane wires a real ruin `FSite` and its id. **A FRESH VERIFIER CAUGHT A REAL BUG IN THE PROBE, FIXED:** the retreat point was computed by extrapolating past `spawnFeet` on the post-spawnFeet line; algebraically identical to the intended point, but on the verifier's run the player settled ~45 m from the post (inside the 60 m leash) instead of the intended ~145 m, so all four guards stayed `engage` forever and no `return`/re-`hold` sample ever appeared. Rewritten to measure both the approach and the retreat as a direct `post + unit(post→spawnFeet) * metres`, with the landed distance asserted IMMEDIATELY after the `standAt` call rather than trusted, plus an explicit engage-to-return TRANSITION check (not just "a `return` sample existed somewhere"). **STILL NOT CONFIRMED LIVE**: this box has not gone quiet enough this session to get any run past the boot handshake without `page.evaluate` hanging until an external timeout kills it (`runner: page.evaluate: Target page, context or browser has been closed`), reproduced four times including on the bare negative-control path, which touches none of the changed code; a build-tooling lane sharing this box is concurrently hardening the smoke harness against exactly this failure signature. `tsc --noEmit`, `check:roles`, `check:proxies` are green. Named for the fresh verifier to re-run once the box (or the hardened harness) allows it.  · **GP-680, 2026-08-13:** the garrison leash was diagnosed and proven CORRECT; the two failing verifier runs were an instrument defect (the probe fixture was killed by the guards and respawned back inside the leash radius, silently undoing its own 90 m retreat). `probes/garrison.js` rewritten to stand at the aggro edge, retreat before contact, re-measure the player every sample, assert the death ledger and follow one creature by id. The refusal was correct; the fixture now places UNTIL it has six and asserts every refusal carried a sentence. (b) `0 -> 11 over 14 aimed rounds` was never zero kills: the 0 is the counter BEFORE the volley. Fourteen rounds landed, eleven bodies died, and the three that did not are one 75 hp Ravager 0.02 m nearer along the ray than the Skitterer aimed at (75->53->31->9, dead on the fourth). `Weapon.fire` takes the nearest sphere the ray enters, so `one round, one Skitterer` was an assumption about the roster. (c) `pool.instances` short by four is RN-123 (c96af0a): the nearest MAX_RIGS creatures are promoted to skinned rigs and release their batch slot, so the identity is `live + standing nests - claimed` (measured 36 = 40 + 4 - 8). No game code changed; `enemies.js` verdict `fails: []` in both combat and the no-combat control. Last updated 2026-08-13

the power wires are drawn for one extra draw call; and the craft menu's icon-less rows went from nine to two, both of which now say WHY. **THE PLAYTEST PASS (GP-53 to GP-56), 2026-07-27:** the sinking is fixed at its root and it was NOT the hypothesis it was briefed as. `StructureBodies.deckUnder` marched down from the feet and clamped its answer to `min(rFrom, r + 0.05)`, which made the floor a function of WHERE THE FEET WERE rather than a property of the structure, so the ground snap ratified each tick's gravity penetration instead of correcting it. Measured on a stationary, grounded, 0.00 m/s player standing on a deck he had walked onto: **0.198989 m of standing spread over 300 ticks, 4 snap-ups at a mean period of 74.3 ticks, sinking 0.002683 m every tick, up to 0.149774 m below the deck's own top face; after, 0.000000 on all four**. The terrain won the `deck > groundR` comparison on **0 of 300 ticks**, so the briefed hypothesis is refuted by measurement. The same clamp had a second victim nobody had reported: a foundation laid on the cell the player stands in puts its whole 0.50 m slab ABOVE the feet, a downward-only march never saw it, and the player stood **0.499993 m below their own first foundation with the port answering nothing on 120 of 120 ticks**. It is now an exact slab intersection of the radial with each box in its own frame, with a rise allowance of one published step so a floor you are standing INSIDE seats you on top of it and a 4 m wall never can. And the space half of the game got an entrance: the assembly bay's launch key was swallowed by a GLOBAL `UI_ALLOWED` and then discarded again by an explicit gate, so a player who built a rocket and pressed the launch key at it got NOTHING; `Input.setUiCapture` now takes a per-panel allowance, the bay has a visible **Roll out G** button, the checklist names the bay and the roll-out from the first minute, and right-click in the bay finally reaches `removeAt`, which had shipped complete with refunds and had no caller but the debug surface. **THE LAUNCH PAD IS A REAL BUILDING (GP-57 to GP-60), 2026-07-27:** the shipped 24 x 24 m pad is placeable, gated behind a new `Launch Facilities` tech, saved, and rockets roll out onto its published `socket_vessel` at a MEASURED 1.84e-10 m, with the pad's four clamps swinging back on the SAME fixed tick the launch clamp releases (938 against 938, stamped by two systems) and a negative control at TWR 0.978 where nothing moves. And the roaming toast is gone from routine production only: the flash path was CHECKED first and carries four things (boot progress, the felled moment, an objective tick, the ingot), the ingot was the only routine one, the cue moved to the machine rather than being deleted (`ingotCues` still rises once per unit, which is what stops a build that had merely gone deaf from passing), and the ledger across the WHOLE session reads **1 banner, 1 objective, 3 ingots**. a solver refusal word (`ok`) that no screen read, so a declined question drew as a confident CANNOT; a repaint key with no planner terms at all; a plan latch that omitted the chosen departure; a probe control that was a tautology; and a press helper that toggled twice. **EXECUTION IS NOT BUILT** and the arm button says so · **AND THEN IT WAS, AND THE BUTTON FLIES THE ROCKET (GP-272 to GP-283), 2026-08-03:** the execution contract went the OTHER WAY this time, published by physics first in `of_ap_api.inc` §21.5 at commit `1e95eec`, and `game/AutopilotRun.ts` binds it: five exports, the 18-word status row typed field for field, symbol-presence detection naming the C names, and a PENDING state that still works. **`MapMode.armed` is DELETED**, because it was a boolean this client kept about itself and the executor is the only place that fact lives. **Driven from the map button in sandbox: asked for a 200 km circular orbit from 100 km, arrived at AP 199974.2 / PE 199972.6 m, e = 0, a mean radius 26.6 m off the request, having spent 144.9069887550107 m/s against a planned 144.90697845865424** (1.0e-5 m/s out), with all four phases seen and **the chart's quote and the armed programme agreeing to the 5% reserve and nothing else (144.907 x 1.05 = 152.152 against a quoted 152.15232738138718)**. Cancel caught a third of the way into burn 1 of 2 reports `wasBurning`, **122.59526912559416 m/s spent and the drawn throttle 1 -> 0**; the burn bar is asserted at the pixel off its own element width, **worst |drawn - sim| 1.20 points over 228 samples with a peak of 99.2%**, and the peak is asserted because an earlier run agreed to 0.00 over 900 samples with a bar at 0% against a spend of 0.0000, which is GP-142's identity trap in this lane's own probe. **THREE DEFECTS FOUND BY DRIVING AND INVISIBLE IN THE SOURCE:** a commanded burn that produces no thrust NEVER ENDS and every field reads healthy while it hangs (900 polls at full throttle, 0.0000 spent, orbit unmoved, because the executor has no verb for staging, routed to physics); the navball's throttle gauge read the player's own mirror, so it showed 0% with the engine at full; and this lane's own warp rule dropped warp ONCE on an edge, which the very next press undid, so the burn ran at 200x with the rule believing it had fired. **Reid's fifth ask was unreachable**: `altKm` had existed since GP-271 with no control on the screen, so "take it to this orbit" offered exactly one orbit for ever. **Both arm doors are driven** (a rendezvous with the station arms `via transfer`, 2 burns, 222.39096829807204 m/s), **the negative control is the state that shipped last night** (only the two `of_ap_arm_*` calls replaced by `const ok = true`: same button, `0 burns, 0.0000 m/s, "nothing armed"`, phases `[0]`, peak throttle 0, 100000.0 m short; revert byte-identical), and **the PENDING state is proven on HEAD's own committed binary**, which carries zero `of_ap_*` symbols and makes the screen name all five exports. Cinder still refuses, with a sentence that is now TRUE (the moon flight works in /core; the bridge builds a two-burn programme with no mid-course correction, so a body armed today would fly its injection and miss) · **AND THE STATION'S DOCK SOCKET BECAME A FRAME, AND THE MOON STOPPED SETTING AN IMPOSSIBLE FIRST TASK (GP-284 to GP-286), 2026-08-03:** `learnStationSockets` read every socket's rotation out of the glb and dropped it, which was harmless until docking became real, because two hulls meeting nose to nose and nose to tail have identical socket POSITIONS. The frame is now `{pos, face, roll}` read from the same matrix columns the asset lane's own gate reads, and **`probes/stationdock.js` measures `socket_dock` at face dot 1, roll dot 1, perpendicularity 0** against `contracts.json`, predicted off the shipped bytes first. The control is the state that shipped and it goes red on exactly the three axis checks while every position check stays green. **`Harvest a tree` was the first line a player read on an airless moon with no trees**, so the checklist parked and every row behind it was unreachable; a card this world cannot satisfy is now drawn, explained and stepped past, driven on BOTH bodies because neither run alone can distinguish the fix from marking everything or nothing moot. **Cinder's four enemy nests are measured (4 on Forge, 4 on Cinder, no body test anywhere) and routed to Admin with a recommendation rather than deleted** · **CINDER IS LIFELESS AND THE BUILD PREVIEW STOPPED SWALLOWING THE PLAYER (GP-287 to GP-289), 2026-08-03:** the plant invariant is generalised to all native life on Admin's ruling, refusing and NAMING rather than silently seeding zero (**Forge 4 nests, Cinder 0, 0 waves, 0 alive**, driven on both bodies with the antecedent asserted first because without `?combat=1` zero nests is DW-31 and not vacuum), and the refusal calls itself a DECISION so Reid can overrule it. **Reid's build-ghost report is fixed and it was not the cause anyone would have guessed**: measured across a pitch sweep, **-60 deg put the preview 1.645 m away with the player INSIDE it and -85 deg 1.385 m**, on genuine ground hits, because looking down puts the building where you stand and a `DoubleSide` slab you are within is the viewport. Two wrong fixes came first, and the measurement that killed the first is that **the aim march had NEVER hit anything on flat ground** (6.014 m is `FALLBACK_M` to the millimetre across a 600 km body). Now no pitch encloses the player, the same silent fallback was swept out of all three placements, and **you can no longer build directly under your own feet, which is flagged as a semantics change** · **THE MOON IS A DESTINATION (GP-290 to GP-292), 2026-08-03:** R71 and R72 closed, so Cinder's refusal sentence is DELETED rather than reworded and the row became a destination with no consumer changing. A world is a different KIND of question, not a different row: `BodyDestination` carries a body id and a capture altitude and nothing else, because a body armed through the ORBIT door plans a **two-burn rendezvous with the moon's centre at 1561.330 m/s against 200 km of rock, and it runs**. Driven from the map: **armed `via body`, 3 burns, 1318.7040652085411 m/s**. **It is flyable and not yet timeable and the screen says both**, with no chart drawn (a flat one would look like an answer) and the measured cost of the gap ON SCREEN: **1721 m/s against 1119 for the same trip at a better moment, both arming and both flying**. And the VAB complaint finally has a cause: **a joint between two parts is not a place**, so inserting into a finished stack is unrepresentable rather than hard, which is why two lanes fixed something real and Reid's report survived both · **THE BAY CAN CHANGE ITS MIND, AND THE MOON HAS A CHART (GP-293 to GP-295), 2026-08-03:** insertion needed NOTHING from physics, because a design is a flat array with parent indices and `fromJson` already rebuilds one, so it is a splice plus the rebuild GP-148 proved. **`[pod, tank, engine]` at 6.100 m becomes `[pod, autopilot, tank, engine]` at 6.400 m, growing 0.3000000000000007 m, which is the module's own height**, with nothing orphaned and a clean save round trip. **The fixture is the deliverable**: two lanes fixed something real and the complaint survived both because every VAB fixture builds once from nothing in a single pass, and one that never revises cannot exhibit a defect in revising. And R74 gave the moon its own curve: **64 samples, 39 solved, 39 drawn points so the 25 refusals are gaps, and the five legs summing to the total with an error of exactly 0** (the check that caught two exports disagreeing by 53.1 m/s). The NOT YET TIMEABLE band discharged itself · **AND A SEAM IS SOMETHING YOU CAN AIM AT (GP-296), 2026-08-03:** the splice had a verb and no gesture, so a player could still point at the joint between two parts and find nothing there, which is the half of Reid's complaint he can actually see. A seam is a node now, carrying BOTH sides of the joint; `fitAt` asks two questions for an insert where it asks one for an attach, so a nose cone can cap a stack and cannot be spliced into one. **Driven through a real aim and a real click: aiming at a seam SNAPS to it (asserted before the click, because a hover that caught some other node would attach normally and the count would still rise) and clicking takes the design from 4 parts to 5, growing 0.300 m** · **AND THE PREVIEW READS AS INSERTION (GP-297)**: the arriving part is drawn at the seam either way, so three cues carry the one fact the picture cannot (amber seam markers so joints are discoverable before hovering, an amber ghost as a THIRD state beside green/red, and a sentence naming what moves), **each with a free-face control asserting the cues are absent there**, because a tint that is on for every ghost says nothing · **AND R73 IS CLOSED (GP-298)**: `holdRoll`'s comment blamed an aerodynamic derivative for a drift that was really a 41.98 deg/s sim inertia error, and **the damper was hiding it**, because `levelWings` rewrites the very field the navball draws. It measures before it corrects now and publishes both readings. **An instrument that silently repairs its own input cannot report on it.** Driven: peak 35.000 deg, damper saturating at exactly its 20.000 deg/s ceiling, settling to 0 · **AND DELETION SAYS WHAT IT WILL DESTROY (GP-299)**: GP-146's first half (order-dependent deletes) was closed by GP-148 and re-verified green at HEAD; its second half, "nothing on screen says why", was never done. With an empty hand the bay's aim line was BLANK, so **the one gesture that destroys work was the only one that said nothing**. It now reads `right click removes Fuel Tank (small) AND THE 1 PART BELOW IT`, counted from the same `subtree` call the removal uses, **with a leaf as the control** so a line that always warns cannot pass · **AND THE WHOLE LOOP WAS PLAYED (GP-300, GP-301)**: spawn to bay to a rocket to INSERTING an autopilot into it to the delete warning counting the revised tree to orbit to a station rendezvous (2 burns, 366.0 m/s) to a moon chart (64 samples, 37 solved, good 1188.30 against bad 2185.68) to the moon armed via body in 3 burns. **And it found the one thing no probe could**: the default requested orbit is the orbit you are already in, so a player's FIRST autopilot press did nothing and reported success. Every autopilot probe written tonight moves the altitude first, precisely so it has something to measure · **THIS EXHAUSTS THE GP-272 TO GP-299 BLOCK** · **THE THREAD PAST THE PAD, THE LENGTH OF THE COAST, AND TWO WAYS OF SAYING NO (GP-350 to GP-353), 2026-08-03:** the checklist ENDED at the launch pad, so a player who did everything the game asked was handed a rocket and no next sentence; three rows follow it now (`Fly it to orbit`, `Open the map`, `Pick a destination on the map`) and **they add no content on purpose**, because what is worth going to the moon FOR is Reid's design question and a hint string is no place to invent one. Wired through a second port set in `bootMap` rather than `Boot`, **null-retires under `?flight=0` and therefore publishes whether it is wired at all**, since a dropped wiring reads on screen as a player who has FINISHED. Driven: **all three FALSE at spawn** (GP-301's lesson applied one night later), **boarding on the pad asserted NOT to be orbit**, then true one act at a time. The panel stopped hiding itself in the cockpit, which it had been doing on the same frame the row became possible. **AND THE PLANNER SAYS HOW LONG THE TRIP IS, WHICH IT NEVER HAD**: the moon reads **`trip takes 10:34:49`** at its cheapest departure against the executor's `light it in 2:16:59`, both drawn, asserted a minute apart so the screen cannot be saying one thing twice; the number was in `of_ap_departure_curve` word 3 all along and the readout dropped it. `playthrough.js`'s `timed out` on the moon leg decomposed: **its 90 s box bought 8177 s of sim against a first ignition 8219 s out**, so it was 42 seconds short and had never hung. **AND THE TWO CHARTS STOPPED LOOKING IDENTICAL WHILE BEHAVING DIFFERENTLY**: the station solves 64 of 64 and the moon 38 of 64, and both answers are right, so the choice made was to make them LOOK different, not behave the same. Refusals are drawn as shaded columns (**6 rects against 6 polyline runs**), the counts are stated on both (`64 of 64 priced / 0 refused` against `38 of 64 priced / 26 refused`), and the affordability rule is always on the chart, pinned to an edge when it is off-scale, **which is the state it used to vanish in: every departure priced and none affordable**, reproduced with a pod-and-engine vehicle reading `aff below`, verdict `never`, arm disabled. Four negative controls, all red by name, all reverted byte-identical. And `probes/mapplanner.js` had been **red at HEAD** since GP-291/GP-295 retired the premise it was asserting, verified against HEAD's own sources before being touched · · **THE MOON IS IN THE MENU (GP-500 to GP-503), 2026-08-03:** an **Another world** group in the cheat panel with one row per body, the body you are standing on **disabled and refusing by name**, and the trip taken as a **page reload into `?body=cinder`** rather than through `of.reboot(1)`, chosen on three measurements, the deciding one being that a rebooted session saves the moon's world under body 0 and eats the player's Forge world (persistence R-BODY-2). Driven end to end by `tools/smoke/worldjump.mjs`: dig on Forge (92 cells / 10 ops), press the real button, land on Cinder at radius 200000 with gravity 1.63 and 0/0 dug, dig there, press home, and Forge comes back at **92/10 exactly**, `bodyHadWorld true`, `otherBodies [1]`, grounded, drift 0.0000 m. Negative control (`worldUrl` returns `href` unchanged): **15 FAIL lines by name and the positive control red at phase 4 of 5**,  reverted byte-identical. · **THE QUALITY-OF-LIFE PASS (GP-550 to GP-557), 2026-08-03, Reid's own priority verbatim ("user experience / quality of life / playability in general"), played in SANDBOX because that is what he is using:** **the first sentence this game shows a player is `aim at one and hold Left click`, and holding it swung EXACTLY ONCE.** Driven through the real key after WALKING to the tree rather than teleporting onto it, **200 frames of a held button gave `swings: 1, granted: 5`**; `Interact.step` fired on the rising edge, so felling one tree was five separate clicks. The decisive argument is not the hint: **`DigAction.step` takes the SAME `use` frame and its own comment says the cooldown makes it repeat, so one button repeated at dirt and did not repeat at a tree.** After, one hold fells it: **5 swings, 25 Wood, `tree felled` on screen**; the control restores the edge latch alone and reproduces `swings: 1, granted: 5` exactly, byte-identical revert, with `impact`, `harvest`, `objectives`, `goalhints` and `cheats` all green. **Four checklist hints read as broken English in the DEFAULT hotbar layout** (`craft a furnace, 2 and Left click place it, E opens it`) because GP-165 derived every key and never gave the derivation a grammar; the phrase moved into the function that knows which branch it took. **Two player-facing reasons cited decision IDs** (`(DW-31 / GP-82)`, `(GP-101)`) and **the Peaceful mode chip read `OFF` in the one mode that is safe by construction**, i.e. the opposite of the world. **REPORTED AND DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED, because each is a design call or a cross-lane blast radius:** sandbox is not applied consistently to the three progression screens and they contradict each other in one session (build menu `free (sandbox)` beside `Stone 0/40`, craft rows red `Iron 0/5` beside a live Craft button, research `0 / 7 unlocked` gating everything while the checklist tells you to research a pad the build menu is already giving away); **the debug HUD is ON by default and covers the left third of every frame, and its last line is the ONLY place the game states its controls**; and **the left button digs the ground silently and permanently (2 to 21 voxel ops in one hold, nothing drawn)** · **QOL PASS ONE LANDED (GP-600 to GP-607), 2026-08-03: SANDBOX NOW TELLS THE TRUTH ABOUT SANDBOX.** The rule is that a drawn NUMBER is information and stays identical in both modes, while a drawn COLOUR is a verdict about what the button will do, so a shortfall sandbox pays for is drawn in a THIRD class and never as a refusal (`costClass` in `ui/GameHud.ts`, `CostText.costText` for the price line, which now quotes the survival price instead of deleting it). The research panel says `0 / 7 researched` plus `sandbox: all 7 available already` and states that the tree gates nothing; the checklist stops ordering a research the mode has waived; unlock lists are NAMES rather than `entity 0x16` and are deduped. **Escape with something in hand now says what it put down, and RIGHT CLICK NO LONGER DEMOLISHES** (it names the key that does, so it is not a dead button either). Two new driven probes, `probes/qolsandbox.js` (19 sandbox checks and 15 survival, two-sided so the survival side proves red is still reachable) and `probes/qolhandsafe.js` (14 checks including a positive control that X still removes), plus three negative controls all red by name with numbers printed and all reverted byte-identical. **STILL OPEN and deliberately so (GP-607): the debug HUD default, the build menu closing after every pick, placement distance, and the belt/port/idle cues, which are BLOCKED on factory-sim D-FS-97.** · **THE ENEMY GARRISON LANDED (GP-95 to GP-98), 2026-08-12: creatures whose cause is a POSITION, not a wave**, holding a post idle, acquiring the player at 30 m, closing, and leashing off at 60 m FROM THE POST to return and hold again; composition is 3 to 6 guards deterministic from a seed, uniform over the live catalogue and deliberately NOT weighted by evolution. Credits nothing to pollution/evolution by construction (never calls into /core's wave loop at all). Takes a plain position + seed today; the POI bridge lane wires a real ruin `FSite` and its id. **A FRESH VERIFIER CAUGHT A REAL BUG IN THE PROBE, FIXED:** the retreat point was computed by extrapolating past `spawnFeet` on the post-spawnFeet line; algebraically identical to the intended point, but on the verifier's run the player settled ~45 m from the post (inside the 60 m leash) instead of the intended ~145 m, so all four guards stayed `engage` forever and no `return`/re-`hold` sample ever appeared. Rewritten to measure both the approach and the retreat as a direct `post + unit(post→spawnFeet) * metres`, with the landed distance asserted IMMEDIATELY after the `standAt` call rather than trusted, plus an explicit engage-to-return TRANSITION check (not just "a `return` sample existed somewhere"). **STILL NOT CONFIRMED LIVE**: this box has not gone quiet enough this session to get any run past the boot handshake without `page.evaluate` hanging until an external timeout kills it (`runner: page.evaluate: Target page, context or browser has been closed`), reproduced four times including on the bare negative-control path, which touches none of the changed code; a build-tooling lane sharing this box is concurrently hardening the smoke harness against exactly this failure signature. `tsc --noEmit`, `check:roles`, `check:proxies` are green. Named for the fresh verifier to re-run once the box (or the hardened harness) allows it.  · **GP-680, 2026-08-13:** the garrison leash was diagnosed and proven CORRECT; the two failing verifier runs were an instrument defect (the probe fixture was killed by the guards and respawned back inside the leash radius, silently undoing its own 90 m retreat). `probes/garrison.js` rewritten to stand at the aggro edge, retreat before contact, re-measure the player every sample, assert the death ledger and follow one creature by id. Both verdicts now `fails: []` (combat and the no-combat negative control). No game code changed. · **THE THREE enemies.js REDS DIAGNOSED AND THE FIXTURE MADE HONEST (GP-690 to GP-692), 2026-08-13:** all three were the probe measuring an older game, and all three fixes are STRONGER assertions, not weaker ones. (a) The ten-press smelter sweep was written 2026-07-27 against a 2.00 m smelter; FS-73 took `FOOTPRINT.smelter` to 4.00 m the next day (aea0d2c), so seven presses came back `too close to #1 smelter` and three machines landed. The refusal was correct; the fixture now places UNTIL it has six and asserts every refusal carried a sentence. (b) `0 -> 11 over 14 aimed rounds` was never zero kills: the 0 is the counter BEFORE the volley. Fourteen rounds landed, eleven bodies died, and the three that did not are one 75 hp Ravager 0.02 m nearer along the ray than the Skitterer aimed at (75->53->31->9, dead on the fourth). `Weapon.fire` takes the nearest sphere the ray enters, so `one round, one Skitterer` was an assumption about the roster. (c) `pool.instances` short by four is RN-123 (c96af0a): the nearest MAX_RIGS creatures are promoted to skinned rigs and release their batch slot, so the identity is `live + standing nests - claimed` (measured 36 = 40 + 4 - 8). No game code changed; `enemies.js` verdict `fails: []` in both combat and the no-combat control. · **STATION FULL-MAP REVEAL SHIPPED (GP-715 to GP-728), 2026-08-13, `lane/station-reveal`:** Reid's two-part ruling, both halves live. On-foot discovery was already wired and is now PINNED (`probes/stationreveal.js` §1 to §3); boarding Anchorage for the first time reveals the WHOLE survey layer for the home body through a new `/core` export, **`of_disc_reveal(layer)`, which is an ABI BUMP TO 25 — the committed `web/wasm/dist` and `web/wasm/test/expected.json` are deliberately NOT updated, so RELEASE MUST REFREEZE BOTH.** Latched by a new `milestones::StationBoarded = 0x0004` so a reload cannot re-fire it; persists through the existing discovery serialization at a measured 100,657-byte cost. 42/42 ctest green. Last updated 2026-08-13
> Read alongside: [MASTER_PLAN](../MASTER_PLAN.md) · [AGENT_ARCHITECTURE](../AGENT_ARCHITECTURE.md) · [ADMIN](ADMIN.md) · **[Phase-1 design: gameplay-phase1.md](../phase1/gameplay-phase1.md)** (the playable-loop design) · [PHASE1-PLAN](../phase1/PHASE1-PLAN.md)

## 1. Mission
Turn the simulation substrate into a *game*: research progression, an exploration/questline that pushes players off-world, loot and structures, and the UI that makes building, flying, and managing legible. Own the player-facing loop.

## 2. Scope & owned subsystems
- **Research / tech tree** · Factorio-style: factory produces science packs → unlock recipes/tech; gate off-world tech behind off-world resources.
- **Exploration & science** · KSP-style science from biomes/anomalies; scanning; discovery rewards.
- **Quests / narrative spine** · a lightweight thread guiding progression across bodies.
- **Loot & structures** · content for world-gen's POIs: ruins, derelicts, blueprints, lore.
- **Player systems** · inventory, hand tools, build/placement UX, vehicle control mapping.
- **UI/HUD** · build menus, logistics/production overview, **map & maneuver-node view** (consumes physics trajectories), power/research dashboards. *(UI is folded here for v1; may graduate to its own controller later · Admin Q3-adjacent.)*
- **Non-goals:** the sims that produce the numbers (factory-sim, physics, world-gen); rendering the 3D scene (→ rendering). Gameplay defines *rules, content, and screens*.

## 3. Key design decisions
| # | Decision | Rationale | Status | Date |
|---|----------|-----------|--------|------|
| GP-650 | A vessel record SAYS which body it is at (`VesselRecord.bodyId`), and every altitude, orbit line and marker is computed against THAT body, never the observer's | `vessels` is a GLOBAL save key by persistence's own ruling (PS-40: the fleet is one world's, not one body's), so records cross to the moon with the player. A global record that does not name its body forces every consumer to assume one, and all of them assumed the observer's: Reid's station read 400 km at home and 800 km on the moon off one unchanged conic. D-014's "one answer to where is this vessel" applied to an altitude | **Accepted · SHIPPED · PROVEN** (`world/VesselBody.ts` is the one reader; `tools/smoke/probes/mapbody.js` green, with a computed negative control that the old arithmetic prints 800,000 m) | 2026-08-11 |
| GP-651 | The map's body is a THUNK onto `SurfaceOracle.body`, never a boot-time copy, and the map's globe is sized from the live body | `WorldSession.reboot` swaps the body under a running client and rebuilds none of the map. Measured: `globeRadiusUnits` 5.937 before and 5.937 after `of.reboot(1)`, and a focus option still called "Forge" on Cinder. The oracle is the one holder the session already RE-SEATS, so reading it needs no subscription and cannot go stale | **Accepted · SHIPPED · PROVEN** (globe 5.937 -> 1.9475 -> 5.937 across a round trip, `globeSource` proving the world's own planet is still borrowed on the body it was built for) | 2026-08-11 |
| GP-1 | Research = produce science items in factory → unlock (Factorio model) | Ties progression to automation depth | **Accepted · REALIZED in code** (`research.h` `ResearchState::tryResearch`: spend science `ItemStack`s from an `Inventory` → unlock recipes/entities; all-or-nothing, idempotent, monotonic) | 2026-06-15 |
| GP-2 | Off-world resources gate mid/late tech | Forces the KSP×Factorio fusion: you *must* leave the planet | **Accepted · REALIZED + PROVEN** (`CinderiteRefining` tech costs a Cinderite-derived science pack; unresearchable with only Forge science, researchable once the off-world item is in the pool · `research_tests` asserts the gate) | 2026-06-15 |
| GP-3 | Exploration science from biomes/anomalies + loot from procedural POIs | Dual incentive (KSP + Subnautica/NMS) | Proposed | 2026-06-14 |
| GP-162 | **A pointer lock lost without the game asking IS the Escape press.** `Input.ts` turns an unrequested `pointerlockchange` into the cancel binding held for 3 sampled frames; `setUiCapture` marks its own exit with `expectUnlock` so a panel opening is never a press. Alt-tab therefore also opens the menu, deliberately | The browser consumes Escape as its own unlock gesture and the Pointer Lock spec forbids delivering the keydown, so the state a player is always in was the one state the menu could not be opened from, and every prior probe drove the action tape below the browser. `escapelock.mjs` measured all three shapes and is the negative control (red by name at HEAD) | **Accepted, shipped** (`0231c91`) | 2026-07-30 |
| GP-163 | **E opens every machine that has a screen, whatever is in the hand** (`MachineScreen.openableMachine`, one set). The quick verbs E ran before (refuel, feed, collect) live on as the panel's own buttons; belts keep FS-28's take-one; GP-61's bare-hand click unchanged | The only door into a factory machine's screen was a bare-hand left click, so a player with a part selected had NO path to the recipe menu: their click was a placement refusal and their E was silent. Measured before fixing; the three briefed FS-92/FS-93 pick suspects were acquitted by a walk-in, click-open, recipe-set drive. `machineopen.js` opens all four machine kinds from standing reach, all red at HEAD | **Accepted, shipped** (`7317df8`) | 2026-07-30 |
| GP-164 | **A crosshair chip may not advertise a verb the key does not run.** Unset assembler says NO RECIPE and offers "E open and pick a recipe"; chest stops claiming to be "working"; "E to take N" became "N ready / E open" | GP-140's failure family, found live on the machine Reid stood at. Copy lives in `FactoryReport.ts` (factory lane file, Admin-sanctioned crossing, needs their sync) | **Accepted, shipped** (`7317df8`) | 2026-07-30 |
| GP-165 | **A checklist hint is a FUNCTION of the live game**: keys spelled only by `labelOf`, slot numbers read only off the live editable hotbar, the panel diff key carrying the resolved hint | The first six hints carried five wrong controls, all hardcoded prose two remaps and a hotbar rework walked away from; fourth wrong-key-on-screen incident. `goalhints.js` pins the table, asserts the DRAWN hint, then moves the drill and watches the hint follow, which prose cannot pass | **Accepted, shipped** (`8183a42`) | 2026-07-30 |
| GP-166 | **A HUD diff key may not initialise to a reachable state.** `GameHud.lastCarry` starts at a sentinel, so the empty-pack summary (the state that names the Tab key for a new player) draws on frame one | The pack summary had never rendered on a fresh spawn: empty pack's key IS the empty string it initialised to. GP-137's built-once family; found as a wordless rectangle in a spawn screenshot | **Accepted, shipped** (`490baa9`) | 2026-07-30 |
| GP-167 | **Visit site: the WG-55 survey's seven candidate spawns are buttons in the cheat panel** (`app/VisitSites.ts`, routed through `Cheats.press`, marks the save assisted). Each row carries the one line that makes its site a different place, off world-gen.md 6.1/6.2: best-ever noon sun (hills2 89.5 deg down to beach2's permanent 9.3), the treeline (hills above it, hills2 ON it at 79% bare, beach bare sand), and the ground. The coordinates are COPIED from the 6.1 table and the file says so: the survey's numbers were only ever CLI arguments to `sitelook.js`, so the doc is the machine source of record; the probe's independent copy asserting arrival at the survey's own designed height is what catches a mistyped digit. The teleport is a PORT to `s.observer.teleport`, the exact path `__of.teleport` and every site probe drives; no second teleport exists. ABOARD REFUSES with the keys that fix it (`labelOf('board')`/`labelOf('recover')`), because ViewRouter routes to the active source and a vessel's `teleport()` is a designed no-op: an unguarded press would report success and move nothing | Reid asked "can I like teleport to other biomes to check them out somehow", live, while the spawn pick (6.1, his decision) is still pending; the same buttons are the decision tool | **Accepted, shipped** | 2026-07-30 |
| GP-168 | **Arrival closes the menu** through the same `setPause(false)` Escape takes, so pointer, HUD and hotbar come back and the player is looking at the place they asked for. Green: all seven exact to the survey's 0.1 m, drift 0.0000 m, streams 0 to 2 s, biomes 5/4/4/2/1/1/3 as surveyed. Negative control: with ONLY the `teleport(...)` line removed the receipt still says done and the probe goes red by name (`beach: lat 2.00000 vs -35.6028`, `4667.8 m vs survey 12.2 m`); the revert is hash-verified byte-identical (`0ad25c2`). Aboard: rocket built/boarded in-run, all 7 rows DISABLED with the sentence, the verb refuses, the walker moves 0.000000 m | The control proves the receipt-vs-arrival distinction is real, which is the only thing a teleport cheat can lie about | **Accepted, shipped** | 2026-07-30 |

| GP-232 | **The guard order is "there is no station" BEFORE "you are aboard", and the station-off test is `lastStationInstall()` and never `findStation()`** | The record SAVES and the interior does NOT (it is derived at boot), so a world saved with a station and reloaded under `?station=0` has a record and no floor: a check on the record would put the player 400 km up with nothing under him. And the un-actionable refusal goes first, because telling someone to disembark so they can press a button that would refuse anyway is a worse sentence than the true one. And `standAt` leaves `grounded` FALSE on purpose ("whether there is a floor here is exactly what the caller is asking"), so footing is a fact one fixed tick after the press and belongs to the probe, not the receipt. | GP-17 | **GAP-1 (slice-e2e) CLOSED · deposit id unified to ONE type.** `gameplay::DepositId` is now a shared `using = worldgen::DepositId` (the `FDepositNode::Id` type, `uint64`) instead of a parallel narrower `uint32`; `gameplay.h` includes `deposits.h` (acyclic: gameplay→world-gen, deposits never includes gameplay). Added `mineDeposit(worldgen::FDepositNode&, Inventory&, baseRate)` so the slice mining loop consumes the catalog's own `FDepositNode` DIRECTLY (read/decrement `RemainingAmount` in place, identical grade-scaled extraction semantics) · the e2e test's `bridgeNode` truncating bridge is gone, and the deposit id carries verbatim from the catalog through mining into the persistence depletion diff with NO narrowing. Backward-compatible: the `gameplay::DepositNode` overload + `Builder`/`findDeposit` unchanged; `gameplay_tests`/`persistence_tests` green (literal ids fit `uint64`). | A uint64 catalog key silently truncated to uint32 at the gameplay/persistence boundary was a latent save-key collision risk and forced a bridge struct in the loop. One shared `using` + a direct-`FDepositNode` mining overload removes both, making mine→persist a clean typed hand-off. | Accepted | 2026-06-15 |
| GP-18 | **GAP-3 (slice-e2e) CLOSED · science recipes authored as data.** Added to `research.h` (in the reopened `gameplay::recipes` namespace, ids `0x0120+` mirroring the `0x0020+` science-item block): `CraftAutomationScience` (Ferrite plate → AutomationScience, basic) and the OFF-WORLD `RefineCinderScience` (Cinderite → CinderScience). Each is a `RecipeDef` (→ `factory::Recipe` via `toFactoryRecipe()`) registered through the new additive `SliceRegistry::registerRecipe` extension point (same append-only discipline as `registerItem`); `RegisterScienceRecipes(reg)` registers both. `RefineCinderScience` is the conversion the off-world gate (GP-2) needs · CinderScience can ONLY be refined from Cinderite, which is Cinder-only (WG-4), so the mine-Cinder → refine → research-off-world-tech chain is now real content the factory crafts (proven in `slice_e2e`: the refinery outputs CinderScience, `producedCountOf(CinderScience) > 0`). | The off-world *science item* existed and the tech costed it, but the recipe turning mined Cinderite into that science was never authored · the e2e capstone had to credit the science off-table. Authoring it as data (GP-12) closes the content gap so the off-world payoff is a genuine factory output, not a test fixture. | Accepted | 2026-06-15 |
| GP-19 | **WP1 primitive-survival slice authored additively on `gameplay.h`/`deposits.h`; the furnace is a FOCUSED gameplay-layer type, NOT a factory-sim machine.** New content (all append-only, pinned ids untouched): 13 items at `0x0030+` (7 raw · wood/stone/coal/raw_iron/raw_copper/water/oil; 2 ingots · iron/copper; 2 tools · crude_pickaxe/crude_axe; 2 structures · primitive_furnace/smelter), 2 entity types `0x30+`, 2 fuel-driven smelt recipes `0x0130+`, registered via `RegisterSurvivalContent(reg)` (same additive `registerItem`/`registerRecipe` discipline as the science layer). Mechanics: **(a)** `worldgen::survival::LayoutTestArea` lays out a SMALL deterministic test-area patch of terrestrial nodes (one per `NodeKind`; position-hashed from seed+index, surface-snapped) · NOT a planet-wide pass; **(b)** `survival::harvestNode(FDepositNode&, NodeKind, Inventory&, baseYield, toolYield)` · hand harvest ALWAYS works (no bootstrap deadlock), the matching tool (axe→wood, pickaxe→stone/coal/ore; water/oil need none) raises yield (`assistingToolFor`); **(c)** `survival::HandCrafter::craft(CraftRecipe, Inventory&)` · multi-input bill-of-materials, ALL-OR-NOTHING (tools/furnace/smelter are made this way; `handRecipes()` lists them); **(d)** `survival::Furnace{FurnaceTier}` · a tick-driven smelter holding an ore buffer + a **fuel-burn-time pool** (`loadOre`/`loadFuel`/`tick`/`run`/`takeOutput`); each progressing tick burns one fuel tick, ore→ingot at the tier's `ticksPerSmelt` (furnace 180, smelter 60). **The furnace is its own type, NOT a `factory::FactorySim` machine**, because factory-sim gates throughput on a POWER NETWORK (supply/demand→brownout) whereas survival smelting is gated by a consumable SOLID-FUEL pool with no power representation · bolting fuel onto the SoA power model would distort the hot path, so a small self-contained `Furnace` is cleaner and the only furnace↔smelter difference is one data parameter (`ticksPerSmelt`). FUEL MODEL: `fuelTicksPerUnit` · wood=360, coal=1440, so coal yields more smelts/unit (furnace: wood ~2, coal ~8) and the faster smelter gets more smelts/unit of the same fuel. Tested by `survival_slice_tests` (12 tests / 1246 checks). | Survival-crafting is the WP1 vertical slice; authoring it additively keeps the pinned slice/science content + id space untouched (GP-12/GP-13), and the dedicated furnace type avoids distorting factory-sim's deterministic power/brownout SoA model with an orthogonal fuel mechanic. | Accepted | 2026-06-15 |
| GP-20 | **An ore deposit is a PATCH OF GROUND with ONE pool, and a mining drill is placed on top of it** (`deposits.h` §P + `gameplay.h` §S.5, web `OrePatches`/`OreField`, ABI 3, DW-25). A patch is an irregular lobed area 6 to 11 m across, deterministic from the world seed, with coverage falling from a rich centre to a thin rim; that ONE coverage function is both the drill's units-per-second multiplier and the tint the ground is drawn with. Outcrops (the pieces that break the surface) are ordinary `/core` harvest nodes LINKED to the patch, so hand mining is the same `harvestNode` call a tree takes, with the patch's pool handed in and exactly what was removed taken back out of it. Placing a drill off every patch is REFUSED with "you cannot place a drill here, there is no ore" on the ghost before the key is pressed; several drills on one patch is allowed. Balance is authored data in §P: `kUnitsPerSquareMetreM2` 16, hand yields 3 bare / 9 with the pickaxe, `kDrillUnitsPerSec` 3.0, radius 6 to 11 m. | The deposit was a boulder on the grass and that fails three ways at once: a player cannot tell a resource from scenery (the complaint that started the work), a machine binding to the nearest pebble within 3.2 m is not standing ON anything so "put a drill on the ore" has no referent, and a pebble has no interior so richness, extent and the choice of WHERE to build cannot exist. A patch gives all three and costs one draw call. The refusal is the teaching moment, the same argument as DW-24. Bare hands always paying is the no-bootstrap-deadlock invariant (GP-19) restated where it matters most: a drill is the thing you cannot build until you have mined by hand. | Accepted | 2026-07-26 |
| GP-21 | **A structure is NOT a `BuildKind`.** `StructureKind {Foundation, Floor, Wall, Door}` is its own enum in `gameplay.h` section S.6, with its own ItemIds (0x0040+), TypeIds (0x40+) and `CraftRecipe` costs, and the factory sim never sees one. | `automation.h`'s `BuildKind` feeds `BuildableNetwork`, which places into `FactorySim` and TICKS it. A foundation never ticks, holds nothing, has no ports and draws no power, so putting it in that enum would force every switch in the factory sim to grow a case that does nothing, would put non-ticking rows into the tick loop's SoA arrays for as long as a base stands, and would make the render key (the dense entity index) carry hundreds of entities the simulation has no opinion about. The precedent is set and was already right: GP-19 made the survival `Furnace` a gameplay-layer type rather than a factory-sim machine for the same reason, that an orthogonal mechanic bolted into a hot path distorts the hot path. The cost of the separate kind is one extra `BatchedMesh`; the cost of the shared kind is a permanent tax on the factory tick. | Accepted | 2026-07-26 |
| GP-22 | **A structure snaps to a SITE, a local metric tangent frame anchored on one world lattice cell, NOT to /core's 1 m voxel lattice.** Inside a site the spacing is exactly the module the assets ship; the site is capped at 32 m and a part beyond it founds a new one. Free placement (B) is the same parts with the rounding removed. | Measured, not assumed: one unit step of a /core cell key covers **0.588 to 1.017 m** of ground depending on the axis, because a 1 m body-frame cube grid is cut obliquely by the ground sphere (four sites sampled; the same defect that split a belt run into three, ARCHITECTURE 15.2 item 102). A foundation is a 1.00 m mesh, so on cell centres a 20 x 20 m platform tears open by 0.41 m on one axis and overlaps by 0.02 m on another. The site frame makes adjacent parts meet at **1.176e-12 m**, and the curvature it ignores is r^2/2R = 0.85 mm at the 32 m cap. "Snap to the world grid" is honoured where it means something: the site's ORIGIN is a world lattice cell centre, so a base and a factory share one origin. | Accepted | 2026-07-26 |
| GP-23 | **DW-24's tolerance is ASYMMETRIC and is a number about the levelling TOOL, not about the terrain: FLOAT 0.55 m, BURY = the deck thickness (0.50 m, read off the asset).** A site's plane is founded on the LOWEST ground under its first cell rather than on its centre. | Measuring the terrain gives a comfortable answer: the worst spread across a deck's five footprint points over 400 sampled footprints is 0.0013 and 0.0069 m on two plains and 0.118 and 0.127 m on two slopes. Measuring what Q can deliver does not: it edits whole 1 m voxel cells, so it has a **dead band of half a cell in which it changes nothing at all**, and one press left **0 of 12** refused cells buildable at a 0.22 m tolerance (residual over a levelled disc: p05 -0.536, p50 +0.027, p95 +0.588 m). A tolerance tighter than that dead band makes DW-24's own loop unclosable by construction, however good the terrain is. The two failures are also not the same failure: ground BELOW the base is a gap of daylight under a hovering slab and must stay tight, while ground ABOVE it disappears inside 0.50 m of slab and reads as a pad set into soil, which is what a real foundation looks like. Founding the plane on the low point makes a base lean towards the invisible failure. **FLOAT should come down the day the levelling tool can fill less than a whole cell** (raised to Admin as a cross-domain need). | Accepted | 2026-07-26 |
| GP-24 | **The walker learns about a base through a PORT (`KinematicBody.solids`), resolved after the terrain and before the ground snap; a door's THREE collision boxes stay three, and the leaf gets a derived box that exists only while the door is shut.** | A port rather than an import because a structure rests on the terrain and must never become a second definition of it (DW-26's lesson, which cost five multi-hour bugs). After the terrain and before the snap because a deck is a floor ABOVE the ground: settling onto the terrain first and then noticing the foundation drops the player through their own base for a tick. Three boxes because a convex hull of a doorway is a sealed wall, and the only way to catch that is to make a walk through the opening an assertion, which `probes/build.js` does. The leaf is present-or-absent rather than a swinging collider because DW-12 says there is no physics engine and a kinematic walker cannot honestly resolve a moving panel. | Accepted | 2026-07-26 |
| GP-25 | **Escape closes the top open menu, from a DERIVED registry, and it does NOT fight the browser's own pointer-lock exit.** `ui/ModalStack.ts`: every panel joins the list in its own constructor by extending `Modal`, the app hands each one its own pointer transition through `closer`, and one handler in `GameplayInput.escape` closes the most recently opened. With nothing open Escape drops the part in hand; with nothing in hand it lets the browser's exit stand and REPORTS that it did (`lastEscape`), so the key is never silently a no-op. | The guarantee the player asked for is "whatever is open, this key shuts it", and five per-panel handlers give you four that work and one written after the rule was forgotten. Derivation is the enforcement: `probes/controls.js` walks the live list and FAILS on an entry it cannot open, so a menu added next month cannot escape the guarantee quietly. Not fighting the browser is not politeness but necessity: Chrome rejects a `requestPointerLock` made outside a user gesture and the rejected promise is a console error, which fails every driven probe in the suite (ARCHITECTURE 15.2 item 106). | Accepted | 2026-07-26 |
| GP-26 | **A nine-slot HOTBAR decides what the left button does, and E becomes interact.** `game/Hotbar.ts`, contents authored as data (`DEFAULT_BAR`), chosen with the number keys or the mouse wheel. A slot holds `hand`, a `part`, a `furnace` out of the pack, or nothing. A part places; the bare hand swings at a node and digs; an empty slot does NOTHING. E stops being harvest and becomes open-a-furnace / take-a-machine's-stock / work-a-door. Every consumer asks for an ACTION and never a key (`player/Bindings.ts`), and mouse buttons are codes in the same held set so a tape can click. | The old scheme decided the verb from what happened to be under the crosshair, which is backwards: the HAND decides, and that is the convention the whole genre has taught. The empty slot doing nothing is not pedantry, it is the assertion that proves the hand is really driving, because a build that placed on every click passes every positive test. The binding table exists because roughly twenty probes were pressing E and G by name; a probe that presses `use` survives the next remap and one that presses KeyG has to be found. | Accepted | 2026-07-26 |
| GP-27 | **Machines and belts snap to the SAME metric SITE grid base building uses, not to /core's voxel lattice; holding the left button DRAG-PLACES a run; and a run is a RAMP rather than a staircase.** `game/MachinePlacement.ts` addresses a machine as (site, i, j); the two tangent coordinates are the site's, exact to the module, and the radius is the oracle's, so a belt still follows the ground. The drag turns every tile to point at its successor, fills in cells the crosshair skipped, ends on a reversal rather than turning the tail around, and commits ONCE per tick. | Measured, not assumed, and it is the SAME measurement that condemned the lattice for structures (GP-22). `__of.latticeCell` on the shipped world at the spawn: one unit step of a cell key covers **0.5903, 0.8110 and 1.0167 m** of ground on the three body axes, so 1.00 m belt tiles laid on it overlap by up to **0.41 m**. When the same measurement condemns two systems, fix them with one mechanism: reusing the site frame also means a base and a belt run agree about where a metre starts, which matters the first time anybody runs a belt into a building. Measured after, over a 15-tile dragged run: worst tangential deviation from the module **4.006e-6 m**. The SECOND half of the same complaint survived that fix and had to be found in a screenshot: a belt follows the ground, so upright 1 m tiles on an 11 degree slope step by 0.189 m each and the run reads as a flight of stairs. Tiles now take the run's own 3D direction (`Grid.frameOf`), leaving a 0.018 m kink; `orient` still only yaws and is still right for a machine, which should stand upright whatever the slope. ONE commit per drag tick because a commit rebuilds the /core network and loses whatever is riding the belts, so per-tile commits would silently eat ore as the run was laid. | Accepted | 2026-07-26 |
| GP-28 | **The whole build system reads the LIVE ground, and `snapToGround` takes the edit set as a REQUIRED argument with no default.** `game/Grid.ts`, `game/Machines.ts` and the build ghost's aim march passed a literal 0 for `_of_surface_radius`'s edit set while `game/OrePatches.ts` and `game/Structures.ts` passed the real handle; the ghost's march now goes through `Structures.groundRadius`, the one call site that was always right, and `Machines` takes an edits port. | Standing rule 1 broken inside a single directory, and MEASURED rather than argued (the rendering lane's `probes/beltfloat.js`): the surface under the feet moves 4.00 m after eight strikes, and a -25 degree aim across edited ground put the belt ghost **1.807 m** from where the aim actually meets the ground, which is a cell and a half of targeting error. Now 0.459 m, which is the grid snap itself and not error (half a 1 m cell diagonal is 0.707 m). The HEIGHT half of the same defect was closed incidentally by GP-27, because the site anchor routes through `Structures.groundRadius`. No default on the parameter, because a default is exactly what let three call sites make the same mistake without anyone noticing. | Accepted | 2026-07-26 |
| GP-29 | **SANDBOX IS A MODE WITH ITS OWN SAVE SLOT, and the mode is answered by ONE object with NAMED questions.** `web/src/game/GameMode.ts` holds the only `sandbox === true` test in the client; `ModeRules.freeBuild` and `ModeRules.fullCatalogue` are what every gate asks. Three gates ask it today: a structural part's `/core` cost (`Structures.canAfford`/`pay`), a hand furnace's pack requirement (`Machines.place`, both the check and the spend), and hand crafting (`GameplayActions.craft` GRANTS rather than calling `of_gp_craft`). Machines and belts already cost nothing so they needed no change; research is not wired into the web client at all yet, and asks the same question when it lands. **The save slot is KEYED by mode (`auto` / `auto-sandbox`) and also RECORDS it.** A `SANDBOX` chip is always on screen, outside `GameHud.setVisible`. The menu entry lives at the foot of the Tab panel and RELOADS. | Three arguments, in order of weight. **(1) The autosave is the contaminant, not the loader.** The obvious design is one slot with a `mode` field and a refusal to load a mismatch, and that is exactly half a fix: `Gameplay` autosaves every 20 s on the sim clock, so a survival boot over one shared key would correctly refuse to LOAD the sandbox world and then DESTROY it inside half a minute. Two keys makes contamination structurally impossible; the field is kept as belt and braces because a key that has itself gone wrong (a hand-edited store, a migration) is a different failure and needs a different catch. **(2) Named questions, not a boolean.** DW-31 asks for no cost AND no research gate AND no pack requirement, and research does not exist here yet. `freeBuild` gives the right answer to a branch written next month instead of relying on someone remembering to add `|| sandbox` to it, which is ModalStack's derivation argument applied to progression. **(3) A reload, not a runtime toggle.** A world that spent five minutes in each mode has no honest label to write on its slot, so the mode is fixed at boot and there is deliberately no `sandbox(true)` setter anywhere, including on the debug surface. **The one thing worth carrying forward** is `Structures.affordInCore`: a mode that lifts a rule must publish the answer it is overriding, because "12 foundations placed" is indistinguishable from a broken affordability check. It reads false in BOTH modes with an empty pack while the sandbox placement succeeds, which is DW-26's two-named-shapes rule applied to progression, and it is what lets a single page carry its own negative control. | Accepted | 2026-07-26 |
| GP-30 | **The assembly bay is a MODE, not a panel: it REPLACES the four render passes rather than compositing a fifth.** `Frame.vabActive` clears and draws `scenes.vab` with `rig.vabCam` and returns; the bay owns the pointer through `VabPointer` and hands it back on exit; its panel joins the DERIVED modal stack so Escape closes it with no second handler (GP-25). | A rocket on a stand and a planet at 600 km share no depth range, no lighting and no camera, so compositing them buys nothing but a way for one to seam into the other. It also makes `info().calls` in the bay the BAY'S draw-call count rather than the planet's plus a bit, which is the only way the number means anything: measured 26 calls with an 11-part rocket and 3 with an empty pad, against a 150 budget. The alternative, a big dialog over the world, is what KSP explicitly is not: the VAB is a place you go. | Accepted | 2026-07-26 |
| GP-31 | **The ItemId block for the item form of a part is `0x0050..0x006A`, defined as the pure function `ItemId = 0x0050 + (PartId - 0x0100)`, and the costs are authored in the shim as `CraftRecipe`s payable through `HandCrafter`.** Class L costs about 4x class S. The reference vehicle totals 38 Iron, 8 Copper and 4 Wood. | Physics R9 asked for the allocation and `vessel.h` deliberately has no opinion about cost. A pure function of the PartId is stable, append-only and checkable by inspection, and it deliberately does NOT equal the PartId, because `vessel.h`'s own reason for starting at 0x0100 was that a PartId and an ItemId must never be confusable in a debug print. It is allocated in `web/wasm/of_vessel_api.inc` rather than `gameplay.h` only because this lane may not touch `/core`; it should be PROMOTED the next time `/core` is opened, and until it is, a part item cannot enter the pack, which costs nothing because a part is PAID FOR and placed, exactly the `of_gp_structure_pay` contract. | Accepted | 2026-07-26 |
| GP-32 | **A DECOUPLER, and only a decoupler, may hang under an engine bell.** `VesselNodes` offers a terminator's bottom face as an `interstage` node, and `fitAt` refuses everything but a decoupler there. | The art contract says an engine publishes no `socket_stack_bottom` because nothing may be bolted under a bell, and it is right about structure. But `/core`'s OWN reference vehicle hangs a stack decoupler under the vacuum engine (`test_vessel.cpp`), which is the interstage every KSP stack is built with, and refusing it would have made the SHIPPED fixture unbuildable in the SHIPPED assembly view. The cost is one honest gap: that joint has a socket on the decoupler's side and none on the engine's, so the measurement reports it `unmeasurable` rather than pretending it closed, and `probes/vab.js` asserts there is EXACTLY ONE so a second silent gap cannot appear. Raised as a cross-lane question rather than settled here. | Accepted | 2026-07-26 |
| GP-33 | **Staging is DERIVED on every placement until the player reorders by hand, and then it is theirs.** `of_vs_autostage` rebuilds the table from the tree; the moment a stage arrow is pressed, `handStaged` latches and nothing rewrites it again. | An assembly view that made the player hand-build the stage table before it would show a delta-v number would be unusable, and getting staging wrong by one is the classic KSP failure this screen exists to prevent. But silently re-deriving over a table the player has just edited is worse than not deriving at all, because it discards a decision without saying so. One latch buys both. | Accepted | 2026-07-26 |
| GP-34 | **A design library is NOT world state.** Named designs live in their own `localStorage` store (`VabStore`), not in the world save slot, and the work in progress is written to a reserved key on every change. | A rocket you liked should survive a new world, a mode switch and a slot version bump, which is what a blueprint means and what KSP's craft folder does. Keeping them out of the slot also avoids a second writer inside `SaveGame`'s IndexedDB store. The work-in-progress key is what makes closing the tab mid-build lossless without coupling the bay to the autosave. Deliberately stated because it leaves one balance hole: loading a design neither charges nor refunds, so a saved rocket is a drawing rather than a bill. | Accepted | 2026-07-26 |
