| GP-295 | **THE MOON HAS A CHART, AND THE BAND THAT SAID IT COULD NOT IS DELETED RATHER THAN REWORDED.** R74 landed `of_ap_body_departure_curve` and `of_ap_body_reach` in the same two shapes the orbit chart already uses, so ONE client reader serves the station and the world and the drawn SVG, the schedule and the arm gate are all unchanged. **FLYABLE BUT NOT YET TIMEABLE discharged itself**, which is the second time in two passes that a caveat written as a REASON became unnecessary without anyone editing it. **Three things about this curve are not true of the orbit one and all three are physics' own corrections:** a NaN means THE ARM WILL REFUSE THAT DEPARTURE rather than "no data" (their first chart priced the requested-orbit capture while the arm used the arrival hyperbola's periapsis, quoting confident prices for 22 of 121 departures the arm then refused, with its cheapest ELEVEN samples away), so a gap is drawn as a gap and nothing interpolates across it; ZERO SAMPLES IS A REFUSAL and draws NO ANSWER rather than an empty window; and **words 5 to 9 sum to word 1**, which is the check that caught two exports added in one commit disagreeing by 53.1 m/s about one trip with 6.5 still buried in the plane-change allocation after the obvious half was fixed. Measured from the map: **64 samples, 39 solved, 39 DRAWN POINTS** (so the 25 refusals are gaps and not zeros), legs `[0, 57.920181443494585, 1005.4114642190814, 255.37241954596516, 65.93520326042706]` with a **sum error of exactly 0**, band CAN FLY THIS DEPARTURE, armed `via body` in 3 burns. **GP-291's arm bypass is REMOVED as part of this**: it existed because `chosenFeasible` was false for a world for the same reason it is false for an empty curve, and leaving it in would now let a player arm a departure the chart is drawing as a gap, so one screen would contradict itself. **The cost-of-timing claim survives in the form the chart makes true**: the 1721-against-1119 sentence is still on screen and still asserted, because a dip is only worth finding if you know what missing it costs. | GP-298 | **R73 CLOSED: THE DAMPER STOPS HIDING WHAT IT IS DAMPING, AND ITS COMMENT RETRACTS ITS OWN EVIDENCE.** `holdRoll`'s comment said a vessel "picks up half a degree per second on the way up" and named an aerodynamic derivative as the cause. **The real cause was a sim INERTIA error, peaking at 41.98 deg/s on ninety seconds of ordinary ascent** (physics, PH-165 to PH-169). That is not a rounding drift, it is the shipped rocket rolling hard, and **nobody reported it for months because THIS FUNCTION WAS HIDING IT**: `levelWings` rewrites `right` every tick, so the one instrument that would have shown it was being corrected before it was drawn. Nobody was concealing anything; the damper was doing its job and **its job destroys the evidence**. The general form is worth more than the instance: **an instrument that silently repairs its own input cannot report on it.** So `levelWings` now MEASURES BEFORE IT CORRECTS, unconditionally, and publishes both `rollBeforeHoldDeg` (what the navball would show with no damper) and `rollHeldDegS` (how hard it is working), because reading after the correction, or only when a correction happens, reproduces the concealment in a smaller form. **The function is NOT deletable and its job is now a different one from the one it was written for**: on the reference rocket it has nothing left to do (2.1e-15 deg/s), which is what a fixed bug looks like from here, while an ASYMMETRIC rocket still develops a real 5.55 deg/s from real asymmetric thrust and drag, which is physics rather than a defect and is exactly what a roll damper is for. The old measurement is left in the retraction rather than deleted, because **a comment that quietly stops citing a number reads as though it never had one**. `probes/rollhold.js` asserts the INSTRUMENT rather than the flight and names its own failure mode first (a field present and permanently zero is indistinguishable from a vessel flying straight), so it rolls the vehicle on purpose and requires the reading to MOVE and then to COME BACK: **peak 35.000 deg with the damper saturating at exactly its 20.000 deg/s ceiling, settling to 0**. Throwing gives **exit 1** with `probe: 1 of 15 checks failed` and the failing name printed; the identical shape with nothing failing still gives **exit 0**; the sabotage was reverted byte-identical from a `finally`. **An early bail-out throws too**, because `valid:false` exits 0 and a probe that gives up half way was reporting PASS at the one point where it already KNEW it had measured nothing. Retrofitted into `qolsandbox.js`, `qolhandsafe.js` and `keycollide.js`. **`run.mjs` is deliberately NOT changed**: it is build-tooling's file, another lane has uncommitted work in it, and gating its exit code on `fails` would turn every currently-green run in the repo red at once. **That is the right change and it is Admin's to sequence, not mine to take.** Accepted 2026-08-03. |

| GP-614 | **THE PRICE IS 20 IRON + 30 STONE + 10 COPPER, AND THE JUDGEMENT IN IT IS THE INGREDIENT THAT IS NOT THERE.** `story_line_outline_v1.txt` puts building the station after belts and smelting and before the scanning antenna, so the bill has to be payable by a player who has run a furnace and unpayable by one who has only swung an axe. **Iron 20 is the gate** and it is deliberately a third of the pad's 60: twenty smelts is 3,600 ticks on a primitive furnace, a minute of a furnace's life against the pad's three hours, because this is the rung that teaches "smelt more" and not the one that teaches "automate or give up". **Copper 10 is why you ever mined the other ore**: raw copper has until now been worth digging only for the power branch, so a first-hour player has a copper patch on their map and no reason to touch it; ten ingots makes the station the first thing in the game that wants BOTH metals, which is what "after smelting" means as a bill of materials rather than as a lock. **Stone 30 is the mass**, hand-harvested and less than one foundation. **AND THERE IS NO WOOD IN IT, WHICH IS THE ONE JUDGEMENT WORTH DEFENDING**: wood is the obvious first-hour ingredient and it is the single ingredient this game has a body with NONE of. `StarterContent`'s own invariant refuses to place a tree on an airless body, which is why `OBJECTIVES` grew a `moot` clause for "Harvest a tree" on Cinder. A wood cost here would not make a checklist row moot, **it would make the research screen itself unreachable on a whole class of body**. Stone, iron and copper exist everywhere. `core/tests/test_research_station.cpp` pins this as a PROPERTY and not as three digits: a pack of 100 raw iron, 100 raw copper, 100 wood and 100 stone cannot buy one, and the station's iron is strictly less than the pad's. | GP-615 | **THE RESEARCH KEY IS EXISTENCE-GATED ON A BUILT STATION, NOT PROXIMITY-GATED, AND THAT IS A DECISION RATHER THAN A SHORTCUT.** `ModeRules.researchStationGated` is the **sixth named question** in `GameMode.ts` and the fourth time `freeBuild`'s comment has been right about what would happen: a branch written months later asks by name and gets the right answer instead of somebody remembering to add `|| sandbox`. It is deliberately NOT a reuse of `researchGated`: that one is about AVAILABILITY (which techs the tree has unlocked) and this one is about a BUILDING (whether the screen has a referent in the world at all), and a mode wanting "the whole tree open but you still walk to the bench" needs them apart. **The refusal is a named, player-visible sentence** on the same HUD line CRAFT_BLOCK uses — it names the station, names the build menu by its DERIVED key, and quotes /core's own price — because a refusal that says nothing is indistinguishable from a key that does nothing, which is the complaint that started W11. **Why existence and not proximity:** what the storyline asks is that you BUILD the thing; once you have, J is a shortcut to the screen the building gave you, exactly as M is a shortcut to a map you need not stand on. A proximity rule would make the shortcut useless twenty metres later and would put a second, harder answer beside the one `ResearchStations.built` already gives. **Proximity can tighten later** and the seam is one condition and one sentence; the probe already asserts both halves so a tightening has somewhere to be measured. Sandbox LIFTS it, matching every other gate in that file, and the lift is truth-telling per GP-600: `progress.stationGate` publishes `required`/`built`/`refusal`/`liftedByMode` in both modes, and `refusedResearch` counts the presses turned away, so a gate that swallowed everything cannot look like a gate that worked. | GP-680 | **THE GARRISON LEASH WAS NEVER BROKEN. THE FIXTURE WAS BEING EATEN, AND THE PROBE NEVER LOOKED (diagnosis first, then a probe rewrite; no game code changed).** Two verifier runs of `probes/garrison.js --combat=1` reported that the leash never fires: the player retreated to a measured 90 m from the post, the guards re-caught them at 2.3 m, all 20 samples stayed `engage`, final report `{holding:0, engaging:4, returning:0}`. The trace also carried an impossible triangle, a creature 2.3 m from a player 90 m from the post yet only 42.9 m from that post, which pointed at a frame or stale-handle defect in `EnemyGarrison.ts`. **There was none.** An instrumented run recomputed every quantity from the raw body-frame vectors: `c.post` was byte-identical across all four guards and never mutated (`chPostEqOrig` true at every sample), creature-to-post matched whether measured against the creature's own `post` or the one read at spawn, and the published `distM` matched a recomputation against `host.walker.body.feet` to the centimetre. **What moved was the player.** Four guards on a fixture standing 15 m from the post killed it in about four seconds (150 hp against a roster that reached 24 to 120 dps), and five seconds later `PlayerVitals.respawn` teleported the body to the landing site, which in this fixture is 45.06 m from the post. The ledger proves it: `respawns` steps 0 to 1 on exactly the sample where the player's measured distance to the post drops 89.99 to 45.06. The retreat was undone before the guards had walked 27 m, so the leash was never handed a distance past 60 m and correctly did nothing. All three numbers were true; two of them were measured on opposite sides of a respawn the probe had no assertion for. Fix, entirely in the probe and strictly additive to its assertions: stand at the aggro EDGE (25 m, not 15 m) so the chase is watched before contact rather than during a melee; retreat on proven closing OR the instant any guard is within `reachM + 8` (read off the creature, because the catalogue holds a 1.5 m Skitterer and a 12 m ranged Lancer); re-measure the player's distance to the post EVERY sample instead of trusting the value `standAt` returned once; assert the death ledger by name; and follow ONE creature by id, because a mixed 3.4-to-6 m/s roster made the nearest-body trace show a false return-to-engage flip and could have let the engage-to-return check be satisfied by two different guards. Verdict now `fails: []`, one guard traced cleanly out to 57.5 m, `return` at 60, home to 0.2 m, `hold`, whole roster `{holding:4, engaging:0, returning:0}`. | This is NUMBERS.md's central ratio again and the fourth garrison-adjacent instance of it: the defect was in the instrument, not the system, and the instrument's own missing assertion is what made a correct system look broken for two runs. The specific lesson worth carrying: **a probe that teleports the player must assert the player STAYED, because this game has a rule that moves the player without being asked.** The nearest-body trace bug is the second lesson: an aggregate picked fresh each sample is not a time series. | Accepted | 2026-08-13 |

| GP-715 | **ON-FOOT DISCOVERY WAS NEVER BROKEN, AND THE MEASUREMENT IS THE DELIVERABLE.** Reid's "you should still be able to discover the map as you run around" was investigated before anything was changed: `MapMode.frame` already feeds `disc.step` from `this.d.player()` on the walker branch, every frame, map open or shut, and nothing needed fixing. What the measurement DID establish is the number nobody had: **at foot scale the SURVEY layer — the layer the map shades — moves about once per 9,375 m, because that is the survey cell and the walker does 4.573 m/s.** So "discovering as you run around" is really the EXPLORE layer (292.97 m cells, 226 cells discovered by a single standing observation inside its 1,996 m horizon disc), and the coarse map fills in only over kilometres. That is discovery.h's design working as written (height buys extent, costs resolution), not a defect, **but if Reid wants the map to visibly fill in on foot the dial is `surveyCellTargetM` and it is already data** (`of_disc_configure`, no code path to touch). Accepted 2026-08-13. |
| GP-716 | **THE FULL REVEAL IS A NEW `/core` EXPORT, `of_disc_reveal(layer)`, AND THAT IS AN ABI BUMP TO 25.** `discovery.h` gained `DiscoveryGrid::fillAll()` and `WorldDiscovery::reveal(Layer)`. **The alternative — six `of_disc_observe` calls from an impossible altitude — was rejected on three measured grounds**, all recorded in the header: one observe can never exceed a HEMISPHERE (`cosMin` bottoms out at R/(R+h) → 0); six passes would drag the EXPLORE layer's capped 10 km disc to six places nobody has walked, handing out ore detail; and a hemispherical flood visits ~49k cells against the 65,536 `maxCellsPerPass` budget, so on any body bigger than Forge it would reveal PART of the world and set a flag about it. Keys come out strictly ascending for free (`packKey` puts face in bits 56..58, i in 28..55, j in 0..27, and `resolveBits` caps bits at 24), which is the invariant every `binary_search` in the file depends on. **SURVEY ONLY** — the explore layer is left alone, because revealing it would hand the player every deposit on the planet and destroy the "the ore in the next valley stays yours to find" half of the design. Five new tests in `test_discovery.cpp` §6; 42/42 ctest suites green. **RELEASE MUST REFREEZE `web/wasm/dist` AND `web/wasm/test/expected.json`:** both are tracked, both are deliberately left at ABI 24 in this commit per the lane rule, so `parity.mjs` will report `abi 25 vs fixture 24` until they are regenerated. Accepted 2026-08-13. |
| GP-717 | **THE TRIGGER IS MEMBERSHIP (`ride.carrier`), NOT THE BOARDING DECISION, NOT THE MENU PRESS, NOT "WALKED ON THE DECK".** `BoardingRule.decide`'s returned `'board'` is only half the routes aboard: `seatOnStationDeck` calls `seat.board(frame)` DIRECTLY, so the `visit:station` press and the flight arrival never produce that decision. The menu press alone would skip the hand-flown arrival the storyline says is the point; "walked the deck" would need a second definition of the deck next to the 57-box bound `CarrierMounts` already publishes (CE-39). **A rising edge AND a milestone, which is not belt and braces:** the edge alone re-fires because `WorldSession.reboot` releases the rider and CE-40 re-boards them, so `milestones::StationBoarded = 0x0004` is the latch — and it is the right latch because the LOAD path restores milestones through `research.earn` and never through `grantMilestone`, so a reload comes back with it HELD and nothing re-runs. **The field is fetched BEFORE the latch is spent**, because `grantMilestone` is a one-way door and a `?flight=0` boot has `Services.map === null`: granting first would burn the only edge the feature has and leave the player permanently unable to earn a map they did the work for. Lives in its own file, `web/src/app/StationReveal.ts`, called from `main.ts`: `Systems.ts` is the semantically right home and is the precedent this followed, but it sits at 399 lines against ARCHITECTURE.md §2.2's 400-line hard cap, and the feature took it to 462 — the 47th `check-limits` violation. Extracted instead; the repo stays at its existing 45. Accepted 2026-08-13. |

Post-fix the moon arrives EMPTY at R200000 on its own 12,500 m lattice and each body's stream returns byte-identical across the switch. **"Home body only" is now a choice rather than the only expressible thing**, and the milestone latch is what enforces it; the moon's map still belongs to the moon scan. `stationreveal.js` §8 keeps the measurement and gained one assertion; the gate is `probes/discbody.js`. |

Regressions all green on the same build: `stationboard.js` `fails: []` 29 checks, `milestones.js` `fails: []`, `markers.js` `fails: []`, `posecheck` 14 checks, `check:boot` PASS on a real D3D11 RTX 4060 Ti. **`probes/discovery.js` is RED with 2 fails and it is INHERITED, not caused here** — attributed properly by stashing the lane, rebuilding pristine `origin/main` into `dist-baseline` on port 4370 and re-running: byte-identical fails ("the out fill flip is the LIMB entering the frame ... on-body 3600 -> 3600" and its `back` twin) on both. | GP-720 | **THE REVEAL COSTS THE SAVE ~100 KB AND THAT NUMBER IS PUBLISHED RATHER THAN DISCOVERED LATER.** A fully revealed Forge survey is 98,304 cells and serialises to **100,657 bytes, 1.024 bytes per cell** (pinned in `test_discovery.cpp`'s round-trip test, which prints it). Live in the browser the reported `saveBytes` is ~111 KB because the explore layer has grown too. The delta-varint format handles it well — within a face the delta is 1 — but this is the largest legal set the format will ever carry, and as a `number[]` in the JSON slot it is several times that on disk. Flagged for persistence rather than optimised here: no save field was added and `SAVE_VERSION` does not move, because the reveal writes REAL CELLS into the set walking already fills. Accepted 2026-08-13. |

| GP-746 | **REMOVAL GOES THROUGH THE POPULATION'S OWN `remove()`, WHICH CREDITS THE PACK, AND THE CREDIT IS IMMEDIATELY TAKEN BACK.** Three options were on the table: (a) thread a `credit: boolean` through `Structures`/`Factory`/`Machines`/`LaunchPads` and into `FactoryHand.collectOutput` — five or six files, four widened published signatures, a branch only one caller ever takes; (b) a destruction-only removal path per population — four copies of the most order-sensitive code in the client, the "two authorities for one quantity" defect this project has paid for repeatedly; (c) call `remove()` unchanged and debit exactly what it says it credited. **(c), because the net effect on the pack is PROVABLE and a probe can assert it**: each `remove()` returns what ACTUALLY LANDED (`count - over` after `GameCore.add`, which reports its own overflow), so the take-back cannot over- or under-shoot whether the pack was empty or full. One arithmetic definition of what a building is worth, still the population's; nothing in `Collapse.ts` re-derives a cost. `Wreckage.unrecovered` counts any unit `GameCore.remove` could not find, must be 0, and is published. **The one asymmetry handled explicitly:** `Machines.remove` reports how many ingots it collected but not what they were (the /core handle is gone by then), so `Collapse.fell` reads `furnaceState(handle).outItem` BEFORE the removal. Four short lists once per destruction is not worth a second authority. **The health ROW is not forgotten by `fell`**: `reconcile` runs on the next fixed tick and drops every key whose population no longer holds it, so the row goes through the path that already exists, and that is also what keeps rubble off the target list (`Enemies.biteable` filters `hpOf(key) > 0`). Equal treatment would make letting a wall die cost nothing but the walk, and the whole point of a wall is that losing it hurts; zero would make rubble pure chore, cleared for the footprint and never for the payout. A third is far enough below a half that the gap is FELT on the small ledgers this game deals in (a 5-unit cost pays 2 at a half and 1 at a third) and rounding DOWN means the cheapest things leave nothing worth picking up, which is the right answer for a belt tile. Measured on the shipped fixture: a 12-Wood wall pays 4 and crushes 8; a 40-Stone foundation pays 13 and crushes 27; a free-to-place belt pays nothing and says so. `SCAVENGE_NUM`/`SCAVENGE_DEN` are two exported constants and a rebalance moves nothing else. **`RubbleRow.ledger` carries what the thing was worth INTACT** so the fraction is checkable off the report rather than against a number retyped in a probe (standing rule 11). Clearing takes the DEMOLISH key, not interact: "remove a thing and tell me what came back" is what X already means and what `DemolishResult` already has the shape for; interact OPENS things and a pile of broken wall opens nothing. In `pickAim` the pile is picked **after every live player-placed thing** — a wreck sits where a building stood, so it is routinely under a deck or beside a machine, and a press aimed at something that still works must never clear it. Accepted 2026-08-14. |

Topped up with extra bare rock swings before crafting (`bootstrap`/`treeIsAHandful` now `true`). **Recorded, not fixed: coal/iron/copper's `patchFell` reads 0 while `perSwing` still grants 3** (rock alone conserves correctly). **WITHDRAWN by FS-117 (lane/factory-reds, verified in source): this citation was FALSE. | GP-935 to GP-938 | **THE TWO PLACEMENT RESIDUALS FROM GP-915 AND GP-924, BOTH DIAGNOSED AS THE PROBE'S PLATFORM/AIM BEING SHORT RATHER THAN THE GAME'S SUPPORT/OVERLAP RULE BEING WRONG, BOTH NOW GREEN.** All runs on a locally built (`npm ci`, `npm run sync-wasm && npm run sync-assets && node_modules/.bin/vite build`) and LAN-served (`vite preview --port 4931 --host 0.0.0.0 --strictPort`) tree, real D3D11 headless Chrome (`ANGLE (NVIDIA, NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4060 Ti, D3D11)`), killed by PID never image name. **GP-935 to GP-936: `basesnap.js` (GP-915's residual), THE SUPPORT RULE IS RIGHT.** `supported()` (StructurePlacement.ts) requires a deck on one of two specific cells adjacent to a wall's own address (axis 0: `(i,j)` or `(i,j-1)`; axis 1: `(i,j)` or `(i-1,j)`), and it counted correctly: measured live, `deckA` founded at `(0,0)`, `deckB` caught adjacent at `(-1,0)` (i-only), and the failing wall-end run continuation's own ghost read `addr:[-1,1,0,1]` (axis 1) -- needing a deck at `(-1,1)` or `(-2,1)`, NEITHER of which the i-only `deckA`/`deckB` pair, one line of cells, ever laid. The platform was one genuine cell short of what this particular run needs, not a rule defect. **GP-935, fixed:** the missing cell's exact world centre is now computed from the site's own frame (`site.o/east/north/up`, the same arithmetic `StructureGrid.ts`'s `anchorOf` uses) rather than aimed at approximately, and a deck is placed there before the wall-end retry. An early version of this fix teleported the player almost directly ABOVE the computed point (matching the target's own projected lat/lon) and landed a deck over 5 m away, at a wrong cell entirely -- the same ill-conditioned near-vertical aim class this codebase has hit before (GP-289, GP-920 to GP-934's own `aimAt` guard): standing back 3 m first, the way the wall-end code and `pad.js` both already do, resolved it cleanly (`landed at -1,1,0,0,0`, matching the wanted cell exactly). **GP-936, a second, previously-unreachable bug found once the first fix worked:** the code that restores the player's standpoint (needed so the later furnace step still aims from home) ran BEFORE the second wall's own placement press, not after -- dead code until GP-935 made the wall-end ghost `ok:true` for the first time, since before that `runAim` was never set and this path never executed. Fixed by placing the second wall from the wall-end standpoint first, then restoring. **Verified:** `snap.runCaught:"#3 socket_end_l"`, `snap.wallRunM:7.276e-12` (well under the 1e-6 m bound), all six sub-claims true, `valid:true`, 3/3 consecutive runs identical (`advanced.ticks 412` each time, comfortably past the file's own `>400` liveness bar, raised from 394 by the extra genuine placement work). **GP-937 to GP-938: `pad.js` (GP-924's residual), THE OVERLAP RULE IS RIGHT, THE PROBE WAS AIMING THROUGH THE THING IT WAS TRYING TO OVERLAP.** GP-924's own "march-tolerance class, triggered by the pad's tall geometry" guess did not hold on inspection: `padBlockAt` (`LaunchPadModule.ts`) resolves a block from the aim hit point's (east, north) local coordinates ALONE, never its height, so elevation was never the mechanism. Instrumented instead: `LaunchPad.ts` registers a placed pad into the SAME `StructureBodies` `aimHit` (StructurePlacement.ts) marches against for solids, by explicit design (GP-58, one collision set for the walker). So any aim across pad #1's own 24 x 24 m, 28 m-tall footprint hits ITS body before the ray reaches the flat, registry-truth ground the original platform pass read, and an earlier hit is a nearer, genuinely different (east, north) -- a REAL cell, not noise. Measured: five different vantages (the probe's own repeat hill-climb, a 20 m standoff, aiming down the flame trench opening which is deliberately not solid, and the block's own bare corner) all resolved to the SAME wrong block, `[-3,-2,0]` instead of pad #1's own `[-3,-3,0]`, reproducibly. A real player trying to place a second pad exactly overlapping the first would hit the identical wall: you cannot aim through the pad you are trying to overlap. **Fixed (GP-937), and NOT by weakening the control:** the negative control now builds a SECOND, fully-decked 6 x 6 block offset 3 cells north of pad #1's -- `overlapping()` (LaunchPadPlacement.ts) counts any offset under `cells` (6) as "too close", so 3 is comfortably inside the rule, and that block's own centre sits far enough from pad #1's tower and launch table for the aim to be genuinely unobstructed and trustworthy. **GP-938:** the fill uses the SAME technique as the original platform's own repair pass (aim, place, and a bounded repair loop for the cells a tiled crosshair resolves onto a neighbour's top face), 18 new foundations. **Verified:** `second-block ghost: {"ok":false,"reason":"too close to launch pad #1",...}`, `fails:[]`, 3/3 consecutive runs identical, and the rest of the file unregressed through save/reload, vehicle rollout (`onPad:true`, offset 0 m), clamp hold/release and liftoff. **Verification, both files:** `basesnap.js`, `pad.js`, `buildghost.js`, `factoryshot.js`, `level.js` all green; `tsc --noEmit` clean, `vite build` clean, `node --check` clean on both touched files (`basesnap.js`, `pad.js`, the only two with a net diff). **GP-939 to GP-949 free, unused.** Recorded by the lane per rule 5. This explains all five of GP-937's vantages at once: on a finished platform the nearest surface is the deck you stand on, so the branch always fires and the returned point is a function of eye and aim direction alone, with nothing from the world in it. **Measured: one aim, no hill-climb, `[-3,-2,0]` `ok:false` -> `[-3,-3,0]` `ok:true`; platform pass 34 new foundations -> 30, 37 total -> 36.** **GP-966 BUILT, MEASURED, REVERTED.** The principled fix (`Solid.basePart` + an `accept` predicate on `StructureBodies.blocks`/`rayHit`, so the placement march sees only solids a placement can stand on) was built across five files and then **measured on two dists one line apart: identical resolved block and identical ghost JSON in both arms**, because the near hit is discarded before the march result is used. The collider reaches the mesh and the pad IS a tall body to the aim.** `pad.js` now sweeps the full plan and publishes the crown's LOCATION beside its value, which is what would have caught the first number. The retraction does NOT touch GP-968, reproduced independently by the verifier. **The corrected geometry reopened GP-966's own case and it is now measured:** 14 m back, aiming 16.5 m up the tower column, the ray meets the pad at 19.6 m (past the 3.2 m clamp, so the march result is used) and the ghost resolves `[-2,-4,3]` -- a storey read off a vertical face. Mechanism: `Loop.run`'s `drivenAcc` (FS-101, `lane/builddrag-determinism`) now starts at zero and carries between driven calls instead of inheriting live `performance.now()` residue, so `settleHere`'s repeated `of.run(0.25)` calls now deliver a deterministic tick count instead of a wall-clock coin flip. GP-907's "not reproduced standalone, likely a BT-116 batch artifact" attribution was itself wrong (the merge verifier got it red 2/2 pre-FS-101); FS-101 is the real, mechanistic closer. **GP-921: `cantilever.js` negative-control re-aim, CLOSED, SAME CAUSE.** 3/3 standalone runs green, and DETERMINISTIC beyond green: all three advanced the identical 8621 ticks. The file's `sleep = (n) => of.run(n)` and its `of.input.tape` hold-frame walks are downstream of the same driven clock; BT-130's 2-of-2 red was pre-FS-101 residue. **GP-922 to GP-924: `pad.js` (GP-905's residual), RE-DIAGNOSED.** GP-905's "GP-37's socket snap preferring a neighbour's socket over the raw grid answer" attribution DOES NOT HOLD: instrumented at the source (`StructureSnap.ts`'s `nearestSocket`, temporarily, reverted, zero net diff to game code), the failing repair-pass press never caught a socket at all (`sockFound:false`) and the RAW grid answer (`addressAt`, no snap involved) was ALREADY the wrong cell. Real cause, in the PROBE: `pad.js`'s own `aimAt` guards a "target under the feet" case by comparing the FULL 3D eye-to-target distance against 0.5 m, but that distance is at least the eye height (~1.6 m) for any ground-level target, so the guard could never fire for the exact case its own comment describes; the un-clamped pitch solve then ran past its own -82 degree clamp and got clamped there anyway, with an ill-conditioned near-zero-horizontal `atan2` picking an arbitrary yaw, landing ~0.22 m off in an unpredictable direction — enough to resolve a neighbouring cell on ground already tiled edge to edge. **GP-922, fixed:** the guard now measures the HORIZONTAL component of the aim offset (perpendicular to the eye's own radial "up"), which is what "no horizontal component" actually means. **GP-923, fixed, same class, second site in the same file:** the pad's own placement step's comment always said "stand back" but the code never did; even after fixing that and calibrating the aim (measured 0.0003 degrees off target), `padBlockAt`'s march on a fully-tiled deck plane still resolved one cell off, so a small local hill-climb sweep around the calibrated aim was added (same class as GP-760's stand-offs / GP-850's `axisProbe`), which finds the exact block. **Verified: the platform completes 36/36 (previously 35), and the probe now proceeds through pad placement, save/reload, vehicle rollout onto the pad, clamp hold and release, and liftoff — all previously unreachable.** **GP-924, RESIDUAL, RECORDED NOT FIXED:** the negative control "a second pad on the same platform is refused BY OVERLAP" still reads `6 of 36 cells have no foundation` at the wrong block address, immediately after the FIRST pad (a new 28 m-tall structure) is placed there; neither re-running the same hill-climb sweep nor leaving the crosshair untouched resolves it, most likely the same march-tolerance class of issue but triggered by the pad's own tall, non-flat collision geometry rather than a flat deck plane. Not chased further within this lane's effort budget. Verified 3 consecutive standalone runs, identical verdict each time (not flaky): `valid:false`, exactly that one fail. `basesnap.js`, `buildghost.js`, `factoryshot.js` unregressed (`buildghost.js`/`factoryshot.js` green; `basesnap.js` shows its own already-documented GP-915 partial unchanged, `runCaught:null`, nothing new). `tsc --noEmit` clean, `vite build` clean, `node --check` clean on `tools/smoke/probes/pad.js`, the only file with a net diff. **GP-925 to GP-934 free, unused.** Recorded by the lane per rule 5. ## 4. Architecture & approach
- **Progression loop:** explore → find resources/POIs → automate extraction (factory-sim) → produce science → unlock tech → build bigger/leave the planet → repeat off-world. The tech tree is the backbone; quests are signposts on it.
- **Off-world gating:** balance recipes so essential mid-game items need resources only found on the moon/other bodies (world-gen WG-4 resource identity). This is the mechanism that *makes the crossover matter*.
- **Map/maneuver UI:** consume physics' orbital state + predicted trajectories to render an interactive map view with maneuver nodes (KSP's signature UI). One of the highest-value, highest-effort UI pieces.
- **Loot/POIs:** define content tables + rarity; world-gen places, gameplay binds contents + looted-state (a diff to persistence).
- **Data-driven:** recipes, tech, quests, loot tables as data assets so balance iterates without code.

## 5. Interfaces & dependencies

> **Phase-1 detail pinned in [gameplay-phase1.md §8](../phase1/gameplay-phase1.md#8-interfaces--consumed-pinned-and-provided-new).** Below is the controller-level summary.

**Consumes (PINNED Phase-0 contracts · honored verbatim):**
- physics: **`FVesselOrbitalState`** (Mode, DominantFrame, Elements, Position, Velocity, `PredictedPath`, `NextSOITime/Frame` · [spike2 §8.2](../spikes/spike2-physics.md)) for flight HUD + map overlay (*we draw, physics computes*); character↔craft state change ([spike2 §6](../spikes/spike2-physics.md)) for the board/disembark handoff.
- factory-sim: **entity-state stream** (`FFactoryEntityState`/`FBeltFlowState`) + **delta/event stream** (`MachineCompleted`/`PowerStateChanged`/`InventoryChanged`/`EntityPlaced` · [spike3 §6](../spikes/spike3-factory-sim.md)) for feedback, production rates, brownout HUD, objective predicates.
- world-gen: **`FBodyParams`** (altitude/datum) + **`RaycastTerrain`/`GetGroundContact`** (+`SlopeRad`/`SurfaceHardness`, RC-7) for ghost surface projection + slope validity ([spike1-worldgen §4–5](../spikes/spike1-worldgen.md)).
- core-engine: `FUniverseCoord`/`FFrameId`, `IFrameGraph`/`FReferenceFrame.ToUniverse`, `OnSOIChange`, `ISimClock` ([spike1-core §5, §2.3](../spikes/spike1-core-engine.md)).
- persistence: loads/saves gameplay's owned `Persistable` (see Provides).
- rendering: gameplay *requests* camera/overlay modes (build-overlay camera §3.3.3, map overlay §5.3) · confirm "gameplay can request a camera mode" is in rendering's surface.

**Phase-1 cross-domain contracts (full detail [gameplay-phase1.md §8](../phase1/gameplay-phase1.md#8-interfaces--consumed-pinned-and-provided-new)):**
- **C-3 `ItemId`/`FItemDef` (§8.C) · gameplay OWNS · PINNED.** `ItemId = uint16`, opaque, hand-assigned stable ids (GP-13). The SINGLE id space: player inventory + factory machine inventories + deposit→item map + persistence. Consumers hold the `uint16` opaquely. Canonical id block in [phase1 §7.1].
- **C-4 `FGameplayPersistState` (§8.D) · gameplay OWNS · FINALIZED.** Exact fields pinned (avatar pos/rot/`FFrameId`/mode/control-mode/craft, inventory `(ItemId,Count)[]`+hotbar, objective step/flags, `SeenPrompts`). persistence accepts; gameplay implements `IPersistable` for exactly this (C-6).
- **C-5 intents (§8.E) · gameplay PROVIDES · FINALIZED.** All 9 fully-fielded + `QueryEntityInventory` read; each carries `FPlayerId` (GP-14). Awaiting physics + factory-sim field confirmation.
- **C-1 `FDepositNode` (§8.A) · world-gen OWNS** (three-way w/ factory-sim) · **PINNED (WG-11).** `Resource` field IS the gameplay `ItemId` directly (C-1/WG-11: Resource is the ItemId directly · the proposed `DepositTypeId` indirection was dropped; no separate deposit-type id space).
- **C-2 `FRecipeDef`/`FEntityDef` (§8.B) · factory-sim OWNS;** gameplay authored slice content (§7) against the shape. Awaiting factory-sim schema pin.

**Provides (outbound · player intents/commands; sim stays authoritative, D-004-clean):**
- **→ physics:** `ICraftControlIntent`, `ICharacterMoveIntent`, `IBoardIntent`, `IDisembarkIntent`. *(fully fielded, [phase1 §8.E])*
- **→ factory-sim:** `IFactoryBuildIntent`, `IPlaceBeltIntent`, `IRemoveEntityIntent`, `ISetRecipeIntent`, `ITransferItemsIntent` (+ a `QueryEntityInventory` read).
- **→ persistence:** `FGameplayPersistState` (player state only; factory/orbit/terrain diffs belong to their owning domains).
- **Content data (PINNED, [phase1 §7]):** 12-item `ItemId` set (Ferrite ore/plate, Frame part, Cinderite, Combustite + 7 buildables), 5 recipes (`FRecipeDef`), 7 entity defs (`FEntityDef`), slice objective chain · data assets consumed by factory-sim (recipes/entities) + world-gen (deposit types).
> *Tech/quest/loot data and the research-gate version of off-world resources are Phase-2+ (P1-D4) · not provided in the slice.*

**PUBLISHED ASK TO PHYSICS (GP-262, 2026-08-02): the autopilot planning surface, needed by the VAB destination panel and the map planner.** Full signatures, word layouts and rationale live in the header of [`web/src/game/Autopilot.ts`](../../web/src/game/Autopilot.ts), which is committed so the flight lane can read the contract in the repo rather than in a message. Summary: four exports, none storing anything, all pure functions of a handle plus the target's own conic elements, with **no client-supplied mu and no client-supplied body radius** (DW-18). `of_ap_design_reach(v, sma, ecc, inc, lan)` over a VESSEL DESIGN handle answers the bay's gate; `of_ap_flight_reach(f, tDepart, ...target)` answers the same for a live flight at one departure time; `of_ap_departure_curve(f, t0, t1, n, ...target)` is **a sampled curve and not a number**, because Reid's scheduling rule is per-departure-time and is only coherent if reach is a function of when you go; `of_ap_plan(f, tDepart, ...target)` returns `of_mn_plan`'s own 26 words in the same order plus the post-burn state so `of_mn_path` draws the planned conic with no second propagator. Reach rows are 10 words `[ok, dvRequired, dvAvailable, margin, feasible, ascent, planeChange, transfer, arrival, reserve]`; curve rows are 4 `[tDepart, dvRequired, feasible, arrival]`. **Execution (arm / cancel / status) is deliberately NOT asked for**: it commands the vehicle and belongs to the flight lane's own state machine, and this lane will consume whatever shape they publish. **Also consumed and already published: `of_fl_stage_performance` (ABI 22, R44b)**, so any panel of this lane's that says remaining reads the FLIGHT handle and never the design.

**BOUND FROM PHYSICS (GP-272, 2026-08-03): the autopilot EXECUTION surface.** Published by the physics lane first, in `web/wasm/of_ap_api.inc` section 21.5 (PH-156 to PH-158, commit `1e95eec`), which is the specification; `web/src/game/AutopilotRun.ts` is this lane's binding and the only file in the client that knows those five names. `of_ap_arm_hold_orbit(f, targetRadiusM)` and `of_ap_arm_transfer(f, tDepartFromNowS, ...9 target words)` return 1 armed / 0 refused; `of_ap_cancel(f)` disarms AND cuts the throttle; `of_ap_status(f)` returns **18 words** or 0; `of_ap_note(f)` returns a UTF-8 byte count and its sentence is printed VERBATIM and never parsed. **There is no tick call and that is deliberate**: the executor rides inside `of_fl_step` and `of_fl_step_n`, so arming is the whole integration and a warped step flies the programme identically to a real-time one. **ABI stays 22**, additive, symbol-detected exactly as `Autopilot.ts::apMissing` detects the planning half. **This lane's ONE outstanding ask is closing geometry** (range to target and closing speed, the two numbers PH-155 measured to prove rendezvous works and the two the 18 words do not carry); the client computes both today as a subtraction of two states /core produced, and the authority has to move to physics the day a DOCKING terminal exists. **Structural, recorded while it is free:** when docking lands, a leading `terminal` argument on `of_ap_arm_transfer` plus the same number in the status row keeps this client unchanged, where an `of_ap_arm_dock` would be a branch in every screen.

Tested by `core/tests/test_gameplay.cpp` (`gameplay_tests`, all 6 ctest suites green).

- [Phase 1] **WP2.5 primitive-survival VISUAL + PLAYER-FEEL pass (UE) · DONE.** Skinning/feel only (no `/core` or WP1-API change): first-person-default camera with smooth captured mouse-look + **Numpad-3** third-person toggle (fixes the old drag-to-look from the GameMode forcing a cursor), a real (blocky, Mannequin-ready) player model visible in TP, a **meadow** floor (`M_Meadow` noise material + scattered flowers/tufts), and **ore-patch** node visuals (clustered rock chunks per kind, tree/pools) with "+N <resource>" harvest feedback + depletion shrink. Screenshots `docs/screenshots/M2.5_{firstperson,thirdperson,meadow,ore_patches,ore_ring}.png`. **Deferred to Admin-coordinated next pass:** inventory/craft menu UI overhaul, dropped-ore item entities + pickup, mining/tool animations.
- [Phase 1] **WP2.6 SECOND visual pass · real Mannequin + stylized environment (UE) · DONE.** Skinning only (no `/core`/WP1-API change; keeps all WP2.5 camera/input fixes). **(1) Real player:** migrated the engine UE5 Mannequin (`SKM_Manny_Simple` + `SK_Mannequin` skeleton, `ABP_Unarmed` locomotion anim BP, `BS_Idle_Walk_Run` blendspace, mats/textures/rigs) from `Templates/TemplateResources/High/Characters/` into `/Game/Characters/Mannequins/` (filesystem copy · internal refs already `/Game/Characters/...`, resolved clean, no migration tool). `AOFSurvivalCharacter` now sets `SKM_Manny_Simple` + assigns `ABP_Unarmed_C` as AnimClass → idle/walk/run play from CharacterMovement; shown in TP, hidden in FP. **(2) Stylized meadow:** `M_Meadow` rebuilt · three-tone green grass + DESATURATED warm-brown dirt patches (old Voronoi read RED; fixed with thresholded gradient-noise mask, grass-dominant); the sphere-on-stick tree → a real low-poly conifer (tapered trunk + 3 stacked faceted canopy cones); +~420 clustered wildflowers (5 colours) + ~960 grass tufts; sun raked DOWN (was pitched up → unlit). **(3) Faceted ore:** `AOFResourceNode` ore kinds now build procedural low-poly faceted-rock boulders (deformed icosahedra in a `UProceduralMeshComponent`) + emissive mineral crystal accents, via new `M_OreRock` (BaseColor/Metallic/Roughness) + `M_OreCrystal` (emissive); iron=rust+metallic, copper=grey+teal patina, coal=black glossy, stone=grey. Harvest feedback + shrink-as-depleted preserved. Screenshots `docs/screenshots/M2.6_{mannequin_thirdperson,firstperson,meadow,ore_patches}.png`. Mannequin content adds ~98 MB to `ue/Content/`. **Residual:** ABP_Unarmed migrated WITHOUT ControlRig plugin enabled, so foot-IK post-process may be inactive (core locomotion fine); water/oil still flat tinted discs (not glossy); no mining/tool anim (Admin's next phase).
- [Phase 1] **WP2.7 PLAYER MENU · toggleable inventory + crafting + drag-to-move + polish (UE) · DONE.** UI/UX only (no `/core`/WP1-API change; keeps all WP2.5/2.6 camera/input fixes). **(1) Polish:** the red debug-sphere domes drawn around every `AOFResourceNode` (r=250) + `AOFFurnaceActor` (r=300) interact volume are gone · `SetHiddenInGame(true)` + `bDrawOnlyIfSelected` + `SetVisibility(false)` (editor wireframe now only when the actor is selected); the play view is clean. The third-person key now binds **BOTH `EKeys::Three` (top row)** and `EKeys::NumPadThree` (users without an active numpad couldn't toggle before) · PIE-confirmed top-row 3 switches FP↔TP both ways. **(2) Toggleable menu (`UOFPlayerMenuWidget`, built in C++ · no authored WBP):** opened/closed with **Tab / I**; on open shows the cursor + `GameAndUI` input, on close hides cursor + `GameOnly` (restores smooth FP look · the menu is the ONLY time the cursor appears); free-look/move/interact are suppressed while open. **Inventory tab** = a `UUniformGridPanel` of coloured slot tiles (per-item-id hue + name + count from `UOFSurvivalComponent`), live via `OnInventoryChanged`. **Crafting tab** = the `GetHandRecipes()` rows (name + input requirements + a Craft button enabled only when `bCanCraft`, calls `CraftHandRecipe`). **(3) Drag-to-move:** a UE-side ordered slot model on the component (`SlotOrder` + `GetInventorySlots` + `MoveSlot`), reconciled with the live `/core` pack each change so the player's dragged layout persists across harvest/craft; tiles implement UMG drag-detect/drop (`UOFSlotDragDropOp`) → `MoveSlot` swaps slots. **Verified in PIE:** harvest fills the grid; crafting a Crude pickaxe decremented Wood+Raw iron and added the tool live; `MoveSlot(0,3)` swapped tiles; top-row 3 toggles TP; no debug spheres. 2 commits (`821eba4` debug-sphere removal, `5a28f7c` menu+key+drag). Screenshots `docs/screenshots/M2.7_{inventory_menu,crafting_menu,thirdperson_fixed,no_debug_spheres}.png`. **Residual:** the always-on `WBP_SurvivalHUD` text overlay still exists (menu augments it; a trimmed hotbar/health-only HUD line is a future pass); drag is swap-only (no stack-split/partial-drag); water/oil nodes still flat discs; dropped-ore entities + mining/tool anims still deferred to Admin's next pass.
- [Phase 1] **WP2.8 DROPPED-ITEM ENTITIES · physical drops + walk-over pickup + capacity + full-pack mining spill (UE) · DONE.** Item ENTITIES only (no `/core`/WP1-API change; keeps all prior camera/input/menu fixes). **(1) `AOFDroppedItem`:** a physical item lying in the world · holds an `ItemId`(+count) and renders a small per-item **"item skin"** (faceted ore-chunk + mineral colour for ores/coal/stone, a short log for wood, a metal bar/ingot for iron/copper, a crude tool proxy for tools, a tinted blob for water/oil, generic chunk fallback). Spawned with a forward/upward toss so it arcs out. **The survival floor is QueryOnly (no physics body) so a simulating rigid body falls straight through** · the item instead integrates its own ballistic arc + line-traces DOWN (query traces hit the floor) to land + settle (rest z≈11). A per-frame distance scan auto-collects it into a nearby character's pack (`AddItem`) and destroys it, with a ~0.5 s spawn-protection so a just-dropped item isn't instantly re-grabbed; if the pack is full it's left on the ground. `AOFDroppedItem::Spawn(World,ItemId,Count,Loc,TossDir)` is the shared spawn helper (reused by drop-from-menu + mining spill). **(2) Capacity:** the `/core` `Inventory` is ALREADY a fixed-slot pack (20 slots, per-item `stackMax`, `add()` returns overflow), so capacity is *surfaced* not duplicated · `UOFSurvivalComponent` gains `GetSlotCapacity`/`GetUsedSlots`/`IsFull`/`CanAcceptItem` + a `DebugFillInventory`; "full" is a real, testable state. **(3) Drop-from-menu:** three routes, all through the character's `DropItemFromInventory` (remove from pack → spawn entity) · drag a slot tile OUT of the grid onto the world backdrop (`NativeOnDragCancelled`, guarded by a `bDroppedOnSlot` flag so a slot-to-slot rearrange doesn't also drop), click-select a slot + the menu's **Drop 1** button, or press **Q** to quick-drop the selected/first item; the inventory page gains a selected-item footer + Drop button, tiles gain click-select highlight. **(4) Full-pack mining spill:** `UOFSurvivalComponent::HarvestIntoOrDrop` mirrors `/core` `harvestNode`'s yield rule but ALSO extracts the units that don't fit + reports the overflow, and `AOFResourceNode::Interact`→`SpillToGround` spawns one dropped entity per spilled unit fanned around the node, so mining a full pack rains items onto the grass instead of silently leaving them in the node. **Verified in PIE:** 10 items of every kind fell + settled + were all auto-collected on walk-over; pack fills to 20/20 (`IsFull` true); dropping a Wood from the menu removed it (12→11) + spawned an entity; mining an Iron-ore node 3× with a full pack depleted it 250→241 and spawned 9 dropped iron-ore chunks on the ground. 3 commits (`ebc50f6` entity+pickup, `30a7f27` capacity+drop-from-menu, `161a951` full-pack spill). 5 screenshots `docs/screenshots/M2.8_{dropped_items,pickup,menu_inventory,menu_drop,full_inventory_spill}.png`. **Residual:** drops use a custom ballistic settle (no rolling/bounce physics · the world floor is QueryOnly); pickup is auto-on-proximity (no manual key); drop-from-menu spawns one entity per unit; **mining/pickup ANIMATIONS + tool-in-hand skins still deferred to Admin's next pass.**
- [Phase 1] **WP2.9 TOOLS-IN-HAND + HARVEST SWING ANIMATION (UE) · DONE.** Character/anim/mesh only (no `/core`/WP1-API change; keeps all prior camera/input/menu/dropped-item fixes). **(1) Tools in hand:** a new `UProceduralMeshComponent ToolMesh` on `AOFSurvivalCharacter` builds a **pickaxe** (double-spike metal head) or **axe** (flat blade + poll) from flat-shaded boxes/wedges (wooden handle section + metal head section, per-section MID off `M_FactoryColor`). It's attached to the Mannequin's **`hand_r` bone** via `AttachToComponent(..., HandBone)` (no socket asset needed · the bone exists on the Manny skeleton) and laid into the grip with a tuned relative transform (pitch −60°, loc (−8,6,0), scale 1.3 · tuned live in PIE). `EquipToolFor(node)` picks the tool from the node kind · **axe for `Tree`(wood), pickaxe for `Rock`/`CoalSeam`/`IronOre`/`CopperOre`, none for water/oil** · and `SetHeldTool` (re)builds + shows/hides it. Shown in TP, attaches to the capsule at hand height in the blocky-body fallback. The tool is equipped on harvest (E) and persists. **(2) Harvest swing:** `PlaySwing()` plays one of the migrated engine Mannequin **`Attack` AnimSequences** (`MM_Attack_01/02/03`, 1 s each on SK_Mannequin, cycled round-robin) as a **dynamic montage on `DefaultSlot`** (`UAnimInstance::PlaySlotAnimationAsDynamicMontage`, blend 0.12/0.18, rate 1.4×) · no ABP graph edit, no authored montage asset. It overrides the upper/whole body for the ~0.7 s chop then blends out, leaving `ABP_Unarmed` idle/walk/run driving again (CharacterMovement is independent, so the character still WALKS during a swing); locomotion composition confirmed (montage finishes → loco resumes, max-walk-speed 600 intact). Tied into `HarvestNearest` so pressing E equips the right tool AND swings it. **Verified in PIE (third person):** harvesting a tree equipped the axe (`HeldTool.AXE`), harvesting iron/stone/coal/copper equipped the pickaxe (`HeldTool.PICKAXE`), both with 2 mesh sections + visible; `HarvestNearest` returns `is_any_montage_playing()==True` mid-swing on the loco anim instance. 2 commits (`27fa93b` tools-in-hand, Task-2 swing). 3 screenshots `docs/screenshots/M2.9_{tool_in_hand,tool_axe,mining_swing}.png`. **Residual:** swing montage plays on `DefaultSlot` (full-body override for ~0.7 s · fine for a stationary harvest; an upper-body-only slot in `ABP_Unarmed` would let the legs keep walking under the chop); the small pickup-bend nice-to-have was not added; tool is shown on harvest, not gated on owning the crafted tool.

- [WEB] **NEXT: a REACHABILITY gate (GP-56), in three parts, because the measurement says one check cannot cover it.** (1) **Static, cheap, and it is a `scripts/check-*.mjs` sibling:** a published symbol in `src/game`, `src/ui` or `src/player` with no caller outside `src/app/Debug*.ts` fails. **It MUST exclude the debug surface from the caller set or it scores its own scaffolding**: `Vab.removeAt` had a caller, and the caller was `of.vab('remove')`. It needs an allowlist for genuine verification surfaces (`projectNodes`, `clipTimings`, `playTape`), and the honest discriminator for the allowlist is that a verification surface READS and a player verb MUTATES. This catches the `AutoLine.lineItems`/`removeAt` family and nothing else. (2) **Driven, and this is the one that catches the G bug:** for each context in the DERIVED modal list, press every action in `BINDINGS` and record whether anything observable moved; the ones that moved nothing are the silent no-ops, and each must be named in an explicit muted-in-this-context list which the probe reads rather than writes. `probes/controls.js` already walks the modal registry and is the natural home. Note that **all 54 of 54 actions pass a naive "has a consumer" test today, `board` included**, so the static version of this is worthless. (3) **Content, and it is a one-line predicate:** every mode with a key of its own must be named in at least one objective hint. Two of the three complaints were "nothing on screen says this exists", and the objective list is already the registry that would say it.
