### Docking, from the physics lane (2026-08-14, PH-360 to PH-369, D-015)

**Manual docking exists and a player can reach it.** `]` docks with the station when the envelope is open and undocks when latched, on `board`'s one-key-several-meanings precedent. Everything physics owns is done (the capture test, the join, the approach mechanism, the save shape). **Three things are gameplay's and are named here rather than assumed:**

- **THE AVAILABILITY GATE AND THE RESEARCH GATE DO NOT EXIST.** D-015 gives gameplay "part availability, the research gate and the UI". Today the `Docking Port` part is in the catalogue and buildable with no gate at all, and the dock control lights for anyone who fits one. The storyline puts the station visit at the end of the pre-alpha chain, so whether the port is research-gated (and behind what) is a progression call this lane has not made and physics will not make for it.
- **THE CONTROL IS A KEY AND A CHIP, NOT A CLICKABLE BUTTON** (physics R101). `#of-navball` is `pointer-events: none` by design and says so in its own header, and the pointer is locked in flight, so a mouse target needs an explicit opt-in. The shipped precedent is `#of-hotbar.live`'s `pointer-events: auto`. **The publication a button would bind to already exists and is already measured**: `FlightMode.readout().dock` is a `DockPublication` that is never null and always carries a `why` string, and `of.flight('report').dock` carries the same plus the counters. Drawing a button is a UI change in a file physics does not own.
- **`mintStation` STILL SHIPS `emptyDesign()`** (physics PH-366), so D-015's uniform rule ("a vessel can dock if its design contains a port") is not yet true of Anchorage: its port is read off the asset socket `socket_dock` keyed on `isStation`, which is exactly the special case D-015 was written to avoid. Physics did not change it because giving the station parts changes what `promoteVessel` does with the record, and that record is gameplay's. The seam is one function in `app/FlightDock.ts`; the day the station has a real port part it stops branching.

**What the chip already says, so nothing needs re-deriving:** the verdict (`out of range` / `not lined up` / `closing too fast` / `ready to dock` / `docked`), the separation against /core's own 0.60 m capture radius, and the SIGNED closing rate. Measured on a real GPU: `DOCK ]  0.20 / 0.60 m  +0.02`.

## 7. Open questions & risks
- **R1 (Admin Q3) · SETTLED:** first-person on foot / third-person+IVA in craft, via **P1-D1 / GP-6**. Control mapping + camera + handoff designed in [gameplay-phase1.md §1](../phase1/gameplay-phase1.md). Closed.
- **R2 (3D build legibility) · FIRST CUT DONE, playtest-validated:** recovered via **highlighted I/O ports + connection preview + a pulled-back in-world build-overlay camera + a flow/`VisualState` tint mode** (GP-7/GP-8, [§3.3](../phase1/gameplay-phase1.md)). Validate in playtest (P1-D6 / factory-sim R3); cheap escalation = stronger build camera before any flatten-pad feature.
- **R3 (no-transition map) · FIRST CUT DONE:** map = in-world **scaled-space overlay** (no scene swap), drawing physics-supplied `PredictedPath`/`NextSOI` conic; **read-only, no maneuver nodes in the slice** (GP-10, [§5.3](../phase1/gameplay-phase1.md)). Maneuver-node *interaction* deferred to Phase 2 with the research/map UI. Residual: overlay legibility vs live scene → fallback dimmed backdrop (still no swap).
- **R4 · gameplay's side CLOSED; awaiting consumer confirmation.** The contracts gameplay *owns* are pinned/finalized: **C-3 `ItemId`/`FItemDef`** (PINNED), **C-4 `FGameplayPersistState`** (FINALIZED), **C-5 intents** (FINALIZED, provider side). Remaining: world-gen pins **C-1 `FDepositNode`**, factory-sim pins **C-2 `FRecipeDef`/`FEntityDef`** schema (gameplay's slice content §7 is authored against the proposed shape and will confirm against the final), and physics+factory-sim confirm **C-5** field-for-field. None break a pinned Phase-0 contract · routing flagged to Admin in the Completion Report.

- **R5 (NEW, for physics) - TWO `PartDef::asset` STRINGS DO NOT MATCH THE SHIPPED GLB.** `vessel.h` says `LiquidEngineVacuumSmall` and `DecouplerRadial`; `assets/models/dist/rocket/rocket_parts.glb` ships `EngineVacuumSmall` and `RadialDecoupler`. 22 of 24 agree. `PartDef::asset` is documented as "the glb node name, EXACTLY", so the header is the side that is wrong. The client carries a two-entry `ASSET_RENAMES` table in `game/VabView.ts`, named as a bounded debt rather than a second naming authority (DW-26), and `probes/vab.js` FAILS if it grows past two. Two string edits in `vessel.h` delete it.
- **R6 (NEW, for physics) - TWO PARTS SHIP THE SAME DISPLAY NAME.** 0x0102 `TankLiquidSmallLong` and 0x0117 `TankLiquidLarge` are both "Fuel Tank (large)". They are different diameter CLASSES, so the client appends the class letter and the probe asserts no two labels collide. A distinct name in `vessel.h` makes that line a no-op.
- **R7 (NEW, cross-lane: physics and art) - THE REFERENCE VEHICLE HANGS A DECOUPLER UNDER AN ENGINE BELL, WHICH THE ART CONTRACT FORBIDS.** ASSET-SPECS 3.3 gives an engine no `socket_stack_bottom` because nothing may be bolted under a bell; `test_vessel.cpp` attaches a stack decoupler there anyway, and that joint is the interstage every KSP stack is built with. GP-32 allows it for a decoupler ONLY. The cost is that one joint of six has a socket on one side and none on the other, so it is reported `unmeasurable` rather than measured, and the probe asserts there is EXACTLY ONE. Either an engine grows a bottom socket or the reference vehicle stops using one; this lane should not pick.
- **R8 (NEW, for Admin) - THE PART ItemId BLOCK LIVES IN THE SHIM AND SHOULD NOT.** `0x0050..0x006A` is allocated in `web/wasm/of_vessel_api.inc` because this lane may not touch `/core`. It belongs in `gameplay.h`'s pinned table beside every other block (GP-13, physics R9). Until it is promoted, a part item cannot be added to the pack, which costs nothing today because a part is PAID FOR and placed.
- **R9 (NEW, for Admin) - THE BAY HAS NO IN-WORLD ENTRANCE.** It opens on the C key and closes on C or Escape, and that is the whole affordance. DW-29's actual entrance is a launch pad gated behind ground progression, which does not exist at any layer: `rocket/launch_pad.glb` is shipped art with no placement, no cost and no objective. Deliberately not invented here, because the gate is what ties the two halves of the game together and is an Admin sequencing call.

- **R10 CLOSED (GP-118, GP-119), 2026-07-27.** The bay carries a pre-flight verdict and refuses a hopeless design before roll-out. Both rules R10 specified were wrong as written and the probe's own negative control proved it: a zero-engine burn 0 is the NORMAL shape of a staged rocket here and GP-73's vehicle flies with one, so the question asked is whether the first burn THAT HAS ENGINES can lift. See GP-118. **What R10 got exactly right and is worth repeating:** the facts were already on that screen as figures, and a figure nobody reads is not a warning.
- **R11 CLOSED (GP-121), 2026-07-27.** `Clear pad Del` sits beside `Roll out G` in the bay, `recover` is in `VabDeps` (late-bound through the new `VabBoot.ts`, because the bay is built before flight) and in the bay's `setUiCapture` allowance. It was two lines rather than one: the verb returns a BOOLEAN so the bay can answer in its OWN message line, because `setWorldUi(false)` has hidden the flight HUD that `recover` normally speaks through.
- **R12 (MEASURED, for persistence and physics) - A ROCKET STANDING ON A LAUNCH PAD IS NOT IN THE SAVE AT ALL, AND NOW THERE ARE NUMBERS.** PH-30 says the world save is refused while the player is aboard, and `SaveInhibit` explicitly permits saving with a vessel PARKED. `tools/smoke/reload.mjs --setup=probes/padclear.js --setupargs={"mode":"leave"}`: a 7-part vessel standing on a built pad, with a successful autosave written while it stood there (`saves 0 -> 1`, `saveInhibit.refused 0`), comes back from a real browser reload as `flight.live false, flight.parts 0`. The pad itself round-trips exactly (position moved 0 m, `rollouts` 1). So GP-74's clearing cannot resurrect anything, because nothing is resurrected either way, and the open question is the opposite one: a player who builds a rocket, rolls it out and closes the tab loses it silently and gets no warning, because the navball's `NOT_SAVED_NOTE` chip is only shown while ABOARD.

- **FOR ADMIN AND FOR REID, EXPLICITLY REFUSED RATHER THAN GUESSED (GP-350, 2026-08-03) - THE THREAD NOW REACHES THE MAP AND THERE IS STILL NO REASON TO GO.** The checklist's last row asks a player to LOOK at a destination, and it stops there deliberately. What is at the moon (the science, the loot, the off-world resource that gates a recipe, which is MASTER_PLAN section 6's whole crossover mechanism) is a design decision with Reid's name on it, and a lane that answered it inside a hint string would have authored a progression spine by accident and made it expensive to change. **The thread and the reward are separable and only the thread was owed tonight.** When the reward is decided, the row after `where` writes itself and nothing already shipped moves.
- **RECORDED, NOT FIXED (GP-350's neighbour, 2026-08-03) - THE LAUNCH GUIDE SAYS "M OPENS THE MAP" WHILE THE MAP IS OPEN.** Visible in `docs/screenshots/GP352_moonchart.png`: the chip under the navball reads `IN ORBIT. M OPENS THE MAP` on a frame whose whole centre IS the map. `launchStep` takes a `FlightSession` and nothing else, by design (GP-139), and knowing whether the map is up would mean handing it a second authority for one sentence. It is wrong rather than harmful, the map's own panel instructs correctly, and the fix is either a port or a state the session already has; not worth a wiring change at the end of a pass, so it is written down instead.
- **FOR BUILD-TOOLING, OBSERVED IN PASSING (GP-352, 2026-08-03) - `check-limits` FAILS AT HEAD AND HAS FOR SOME TIME.** Run against a clean `git archive HEAD` tree it exits 1 with 49 files over the 400-line cap, `Boot.ts` at 633 among them. **A gate that is red for everybody is a gate nobody reads**, which is INSTRUMENTS.md's "a green result from an instrument that was never running" with the sign flipped: this one is never green, so a new violation is invisible inside it. This pass kept its own files honest by lifting twice (`ObjectiveList.ts`, `MapChart.ts`), and `MapPlannerPanel.ts` still ends at 444 against a pre-existing 425. Whether the cap should be enforced, raised, or scoped to new files is not this lane's call.
- **ROUTED TO PHYSICS (GP-281, 2026-08-03) - A COMMANDED BURN THAT PRODUCES NO THRUST NEVER TERMINATES.** `autopilot.h` cuts a burn on MEASURED delta-v, which is the right rule and is why the orbits come out to metres. It has no verb for staging and no timeout, so a vehicle whose next engine has not been staged sits in `Phase::Burn` at full throttle for ever: measured at **900 consecutive polls, 0.0000 of a planned 144.9070 m/s spent, orbit unmoved, every published field reading healthy**. `remainingDeltaVVacuumMS` counts every stage, so the programme arms as affordable. Not the client's to fix (staging is the vehicle's and the abort is the executor's); the client detects it and names the one keypress that clears it. **Suggested shape: the executor either stages, or aborts with a note, after a burn has delivered nothing for a bounded time.**
- **ROUTED TO PHYSICS (GP-278's neighbour, 2026-08-03) - NOTHING PUBLISHES /core's SAS MODE, so the navball's SAS chip cannot be made truthful the way its throttle just was.** `of_ap_cancel` cuts the throttle and leaves `sim.sas` in `SasMode::Command` at the burn vector, which is arguably the right behaviour (a vehicle that goes slack mid-burn is worse) and is not what is being questioned. The problem is that the chip draws `FlightSession.sasMode`, the client's own copy, which still says whatever the player last chose. `of_fl_state` is 17 words and an eighteenth appended at the end moves no existing index, exactly as `radialOffsetM` arrived at ABI 20. **Deliberately not worked around here**: re-asserting the client's mode after a cancel would change what the vehicle does in order to fix what a chip says.
- **FOR ADMIN, AND IT IS NOT A DEFECT (2026-08-03) - THE EXECUTOR'S OWN REFUSAL IS UNREACHABLE FROM THIS SCREEN BY CONSTRUCTION.** `MapPlannerCtl.planAct`'s `autopilot refused: <note>` branch was never taken in any driven run, and the reason is structural rather than lucky: the chart gate refuses at `dv * 1.05 <= available` while the executor refuses at `dv <= remaining`, so **the upstream gate is strictly stricter by the whole 5% reserve** and the player is kept away from the executor's own no. That is the design working (a disabled button is a hint and a refusal is a rule), and it means the branch is defence in depth rather than a gate with a proven refusing case. Recorded rather than deleted, because the day the two gates disagree the refusal has to reach the player, and recorded rather than left silent, because INSTRUMENTS.md says a gate with no reachable refusing case is decoration and this one has to be argued rather than demonstrated.

- **FOR ADMIN, A DESIGN CALL WITH THE MEASUREMENT ATTACHED (GP-286's sibling, 2026-08-03) - CINDER HAS FOUR ENEMY NESTS AND NOTHING IN THE CODE HAS AN OPINION ABOUT THAT.** Measured: `?combat=1` on Forge seeds **4 nests**, and `?combat=1&body=cinder` seeds **4 nests**, because `EnemyLoop.seedNests` walks `NEST_RING.count` with no body test of any kind. It is not a wrong table, it is an absent question. **MY VIEW, ASKED FOR AND NOT ENACTED: Cinder should be lifeless, and native fauna is the plant invariant with a different noun.** Three reasons. GP-2 is the whole crossover argument, that off-world resources gate mid tech, so the moon is a place you EARN rather than a second starting world, and a tutorial chain plus a hostile ecology on it says the opposite. If Cinder has trees and creatures it is Forge with a different sky, and vacuum with nothing alive in it is the cheapest and strongest way to make arriving there feel like an achievement. And a creature that lives in vacuum needs a story nobody has written (pressurised? machine? something the player MADE?), which is content design and Reid's call rather than a lane's. **What I would build once the call is made:** the same shape as `PLANT_KINDS`, an invariant no table can override, refusing and NAMING rather than silently seeding zero, so the next person to fill Cinder's row cannot put a nest back without the refusal saying so. **What I deliberately did not do:** delete four nests, because "the moon is quiet" and "somebody removed the moon's enemies" are different worlds and only one of them is a decision.
- **SWEPT, AND THE CLASS IS NAMED (GP-286, 2026-08-03).** WG-144's rule is that a fix landing on one body means going and checking the siblings in the same pass, and R16 closed the plant case on Cinder without anybody sweeping for the rest. The class is **"content and copy that assumes Forge"**. Three members found: the tutorial's `wood` card (fixed here), the four nests (routed above), and the duplicated airless test (folded into one authority here). `STARTER` already carries a per-body table and `of_atmo_density` already answers per body, so the sweep is cheap for whoever does the next one: **anything keyed on nothing is the thing to look at, not anything keyed wrong.**

- **REID'S DEFECT, FIXED, AND BOTH HALVES OF HIS REPORT WERE TRUE (GP-400, 2026-08-03) - THE HALL'S TWELVE WALL PROXIES WERE TWELVE BOXES TRANSLATED ROUND A CIRCLE AND NEVER TURNED TO FACE IT.** He reported *"i just tested out teleporting to the space station and walking around. i can move through walls and if i jump i then fall through the floor"*. Physics R56 had recorded the opposite symptom (`col_HallWallA` blocking the whole spine mouth, the walker stopping dead 2.251356 m short), and both readings were correct because **R56 measured along the one axis where the pinwheel happened to point the right way**. `of_lib.add_collision_box(name, size, loc, parent, role)` has no rotation argument, so `for k in range(12): loc = radial(30k) * 8.25` placed twelve identically-shaped 0.30 x 4.536 x 6.00 slabs at twelve azimuths: k = 0 and k = 6 line up with +/-X and over-block, the pair at 90 and 270 are radial fins running from radius 5.982 clean out to 10.518, and the other eight are oblique. Swept at 5 degrees, **24 of the 60 hall azimuths had no wall inside 12 m and the rest met one 1.3 to 3.1 m OUTSIDE the last floor**, so a player walking any heading but the spine ran off the deck before anything stopped them. The floor was the same defect a level down: `col_HallFloor` was the square inscribed in a round room, and `col_HallSill{Fwd,Aft}` bridged it on the CENTRELINE only, which is why R57 read as a 2.099 m hole rather than as the whole perimeter it is.
- **THE FIX IS A STAIRCASE WHOSE FLOOR AND WALL COME OFF ONE LIST, AND THE ANGLES ARE NOT EVENLY SPACED (GP-400).** `hall_steps()` in `build_space_station.py` returns three rectangles with their corners on the circle; the deck is their union and the wall is the risers of that union, extended by the wall's own thickness at the OUTER end of each riser and not at the inner one. That asymmetry is the whole of whether the shell closes: at the outer end the deck beyond is shorter so the extension fills the corner a 0.13 m diagonal step would otherwise slip through, and at the inner end the deck beyond is longer so extending would stand a wall in the middle of walkable floor, which is R56 rebuilt. Because the step angles are symmetric about 45 degrees the x extents are the z extents reversed, so every riser lands exactly on the perpendicular riser of the far axis and no corner leaks. **The angles have a closed form worth keeping:** holding every inside corner at radius Rk makes the squared sines an arithmetic progression, which closes only at `k^2 = M/(M+1)`, so `t_j = asin(sqrt((j+1)/(M+1)))` and the shortfall is `R(1 - sqrt(M/(M+1)))`. Evenly spaced angles dump the error at 45 degrees and stand **2.372 m** inside the hull there, worse than the defect R56 was raised for; equal spacing of the ERROR gives **1.085 m** for the same boxes and the same names.
- **R56 AND R57 ARE CLOSED AND R57 IS RETIRED RATHER THAN BRIDGED (GP-400).** `col_HallSill{Fwd,Aft}` are deleted. The two spine runs now start 50 mm INSIDE the hall's own deck edge and bring their walls and ceiling with them, so there is no vestibule left. The sill was tried first and it opened a new hole: a plank wide enough to floor the join has OPEN SIDES, so a player who walked through the hatch and stepped sideways went off the edge of it, and a plank wide enough to fix THAT puts unwalled deck outside the hall wall. `tools/blender/check_station_deck.py` was already written for this exact question and was **known red at 290 of 360 azimuths (80.6%)**; it is **0 of 2880** now, and it reads the shipped bytes rather than the builder, which is why no second seal check was added to the build.
- **DRIVEN, BECAUSE THE OFFLINE GATE MARCHES ONE RAY FROM ONE POINT (GP-400).** `probes/stationwalk.js` gains **P6**: 24 headings out of the hall, each walking outward, hopping in place, then hopping with the walk key held, with the generator on and the aft vented section held to the geometry test rather than the weight one. Result: **0 of 24 lost, 22 of 24 stopped by a wall, `maxFellM` -0.000004 m, `maxEndBelowDeckM` 0**. The refusing case is the SAME `leg` function run 200 m beside the station, where it reports a 136.388622 m fall and `endOnDeck: false`, and it is asserted BEFORE the thing it licenses. **P4 and P5 both walk `A.along` and only `A.along`**, which is why R56 was recorded as "the walls block" while Reid experienced "the walls do not"; that is the fixture failure this phase exists to retire.
- **THE HARNESS WAS WRONG TWICE BEFORE THE STATION WAS (GP-400, and both were caught by the numbers rather than by the verdict).** First draft gave the hop 1.4 s and the run-and-hop 2.4 s against a **2.30 s airtime** at station gravity, so 8 of 24 legs were still in the air when the leg ended. Second draft then called three legs lost on `endGrounded` while their `fellM` was **-0.000004 m**: `grounded` is a claim about WEIGHT and `KinematicBody` sets it false in freefall deliberately, so it can never be the predicate for "is the player still in the station". The verdict is `onDeck` plus the deck-radius test now. Neither was found by staring at the assertion; both were found because the leg publishes `fellM` and `roseM` beside the boolean.
- **STILL WRONG, WITH THE NUMBER AND THE FIX (GP-400, FOR ADMIN, and it is a COMFORT defect rather than a safety one) - A PLAYER STOPS UP TO 1.085 m INSIDE THE VISIBLE HALL WALL AT EIGHT POINTS ROUND THE ROOM.** The driven sweep measures the tightest stop at **7.012943 m** against the asset's predicted 7.0148, agreeing to 2 mm, so the closed form and the real walker are checked against each other. Nobody falls, because the deck stops exactly where the wall does. **More steps is not the fix**: halving the shortfall doubles the boxes (0.601 m at six steps, 0.295 m at thirteen, `8 * STEPS` wall proxies each time), because an axis-aligned box set cannot follow a circle. **The fix that takes it to zero is a per-proxy ROTATION surviving into the client**, and it is smaller than it sounds: `learnStationProxies` collapses each proxy through `Box3.applyMatrix4`, which is the AABB of the transformed corners, and a `Solid` carries ONE quaternion for its whole box list, so the change is to install one `Solid` PER PROXY FRAME instead of one for the station. **`StructureBodies.inside` and `.deckUnder` already handle an arbitrary `Solid.quat` and need no change at all**; `NamedBox.min`/`max` stay the station-local AABB so `StationGravity` and every probe that reads proxies by name are untouched. With that in place the hall wall becomes the sixteen chords the hull already is and the stand-off is 0.000. `check_station_deck.py` would then need to march the OBB rather than the bound.
- **FOR PHYSICS, AND IT IS A PROBE DEFECT RATHER THAN AN ASSET ONE (GP-400, 2026-08-03) - `probes/zerog.js` Z6 STANDS THE PLAYER INSIDE `col_HallCore`.** Z6 is red at HEAD and red after this pass, **identically** (rise 3.025007 m before, 3.075424 m after, plateau 1.318755 against 1.331255), so it is not a regression, and it is red for the reason `stationwalk.js` P2 was red before PH-105 fixed it: `const hub = at(0.5)` is 0.5 m radially above the station's own position, and the station's own position is inside the solid core column, x and z in [-1.548, 1.548] and 5.4 m tall. `resolveStep` lets a body already inside a solid move freely, by design, so the thrust test runs with no collision at all. **`install.standPos`, derived from `socket_hall`, is the one-line fix and it is the same one PH-105 already applied next door.** Deliberately not taken here: it is physics's probe, and re-aiming it will change what several of its phases measure. **CLOSED 2026-08-15 BY CE-100 (core-engine §5m).** The prescription was right and the caveat was too: the leg is seated at the asset's own `socket_hall` through `of.standAboard()` (the live pose, not the boot record `install.standPos` now is), and re-aiming it did change what several phases measure , Z6's push-off plateaus at **2.65 m** against the old 0.85 m, and Z7's hand-tuned `acrossM` bound had to be replaced by a `solidBuild` scan because the correct walk from the socket legitimately exceeds it.
- **PRE-EXISTING RED, CONFIRMED AGAINST HEAD AND NOT MINE (GP-400):** `probes/stationvisit.js` fails at `the probe got a rocket built, rolled out and boarded: ""` with the old glb restored, so it is a VAB/roll-out precondition rather than the station. `probes/orbitdeck.js` needs `--sandbox=1` and is green with it. `airlock.js`, `stationdock.js`, `stationdraw.js` and `stationwalk.js` P1 to P5 are green on the new asset.
- **THE HALL'S SHELL IS AS TALL AS THE HALL NOW, AND IT WAS 6.00 m OF A 13.30 m ROOM (GP-400).** Height costs no boxes, so the wall is the drum's own `HUB_Z1` and `col_HallCeil{X,,Z}` caps it on the same three rectangles as the deck. Under weight this changes nothing (the jump apex at station gravity is 2.319915 m); with the generator OFF it was a way out, which is R48's "in freefall a ceiling is the normal case". It does not turn Z6 green, and it should not be expected to: Z6 thrusts for 2.0 s and reaches 3.08 m, so a 12.00 m lid is outside its window even from a legal standing point.
- **EXPORT/BUDGET CHANGES, STATED (GP-400):** the station ships **82 proxies against 63**, `max_tris_collision` in `contracts.json` goes **800 to 1000** (984 shipped), and the `collision` name list is rewritten. No `.wasm` change and no `expected.json` change. `web/public/assets/**` is generated by `sync-assets.mjs` and is not committed; the tracked artefact is `assets/models/dist/structures/space_station.glb`.

- **REID'S ASK, SHIPPED (GP-500, 2026-08-03) - THE MOON IS A BUTTON NOW, AND THE DOOR IS A PAGE RELOAD BECAUSE THREE MEASUREMENTS SAID SO RATHER THAN BECAUSE IT WAS EASIER.** Cinder has been fully built for weeks and reachable only by typing `?body=cinder` into the address bar, which is not a door a player has. The runtime path exists (`of.reboot(bodyId)`, core-engine CE-20, teardown 2 ms and rebuild 71 ms with Forge bit-identical after a round trip) and was **refused on evidence**: (1) `of.life().stale` after `of.reboot(1)` names seven holders that still believe in Forge, and five of them are this domain's side of the house, with `gameplay.bodyRadiusM` reading 600000 on a 200 km moon and `gameplay.bodyHandle` reading 1 against a live 2, so every building, rock, tree, health row and site-grid anchor would be placed against the wrong planet through a handle that is not the live one; (2) **persistence R-BODY-2 decides it**, because `slot.body` follows `Gameplay.bodyId`, which boot builds and `WorldSession.reboot` deliberately does not rebuild, so a rebooted session saves the MOON's world under body 0 and destroys the player's Forge world in the slot, which is PS-40's own defect reached through the front door; (3) a reboot does not relocate the player, so the walker keeps Forge's body-frame position, which on a 200 km moon is 400 km up with nothing under it. **A reload into `?body=cinder` is not a new path at all**, so PS-40's per-body save, GP-286's checklist stepping, GP-287's lifeless moon and RN-840's airless sky all work unchanged and none of them has to be re-proven. The cost is a black screen, and the row says so in its own note, because a page that goes white without warning reads as a crash. Files: `web/src/app/VisitWorlds.ts` (new, the whole argument is in its header), `web/src/app/MenuBoot.ts` (`goTo`, `restart`'s sibling, and the save is **awaited** because `pagehide` cannot await two IndexedDB round trips), `Cheats.ts`, `PauseMenu.ts`, `PauseRootHtml.ts`.
- **WHAT IT DOES WHEN YOU ARE ALREADY ON THE MOON, WHICH IS THE QUESTION THE BRIEF ASKED (GP-500).** It refuses by name, and the row for the world you are NOT on is the way back. **One table, two rows, no special case**: `blocked` is `you are already on Cinder` for whichever row is the live `PlanetBody.bodyId`, read live per view and never off a boot copy. A single "go to the moon" button would have shipped a one-way trip AND would have had no reachable refusing case anywhere in its own loop, which is exactly GP-301's shape. The verb refuses too and not only the greyed button, because a greyed row cannot be clicked and a guard living only in `worldRows` would be reachable exactly by `of.cheat`. Measured on Cinder: `world:cinder` returns `refused: you are already on Cinder`, `world:nowhere` returns `no such world: nowhere`, and **not one refusal moved the walker (0.000000 m)**.
- **GP-502: A NEW DESTINATION DOES NOT ONLY ADD A ROW, IT CAN MAKE AN EXISTING ROW'S SENTENCE UNTRUE.** The seven Visit-site rows are WG-55 survey candidates on Forge, each a lat/lon plus a sentence about the sun angle, the treeline and the ground there. A lat/lon is a valid point on any sphere, so `Controller.teleport` would happily land the player somewhere on Cinder with every word of the note false: a desert with no trees, on an airless moon that has no trees anywhere. They are now blocked off-Forge, in `visitRows` **and** in `pressVisit`, with a sentence that names the way back. The positive half is asserted in the same run (**7 rows, 0 disabled on Forge; 7 of 7 disabled on Cinder**), without which "all seven refuse" would pass on a build that blocked them everywhere. **The class is worth carrying: any row whose text asserts something about the world needs a body test the day a second world becomes reachable.**
- **GP-503: DRIVEN, AND IT IS A RUNNER RATHER THAN A PROBE BECAUSE THE GESTURE UNDER TEST DESTROYS THE CONTEXT THAT WOULD REPORT ON IT.** `web/tools/smoke/worldjump.mjs` (new): one browser context for five phases, because only IndexedDB carries anything between bodies. It presses the real `<button>` with a real `PointerEvent`, takes the receipt synchronously before the page goes, then **waits for the client's own navigation** and asserts where it landed, so the claim covers the row, the press, the save, `worldUrl`'s arithmetic and `window.location.assign` rather than the runner's own idea of what `?body=cinder` should be. A runner that navigated to a URL it wrote itself would prove that Cinder boots, which has been true since June, and nothing at all about the button. **Green numbers:** Forge dig 92 cells / 10 ops at lat 2 lon 144, ground 4667.79 m; arrive Cinder `radius 200000`, `gravity 1.63`, `grounded true`, `altM 1.62`, `drift 0.0000 m`, `removedCells 0`, `ops 0`, `bodyHadWorld false`, `otherBodies [0]`; dig 31/3 there; press home; Forge returns **92/10 exactly**, `bodyHadWorld true`, `restoredBody 0`, `otherBodies [1]`, grounded. **Negative control**, `worldUrl` returning `href` unchanged (a URL builder that forgot its one job): **15 FAIL lines by name**, including `THE PLAYER IS ON THE MOON: radius 600000, expected 200000` and `the Forge tunnel did NOT come to the moon`, and the **positive control fired too** (`stopped after phase 4 of 5`). Revert verified byte-identical, with the re-apply in a shell `trap` so an interrupt could not leave the nobble standing. **The positive control is written the one way that can fail:** `stage` advances inside the `try`, once per phase.
- **THE STAND-OFF WITH R-BODY-1, STATED RATHER THAN PROMISED AWAY (GP-503).** `SavePlayerAnchor` is bare lat/lon/alt with no body id, so a Forge anchor restores at the same lat/lon on a 200 km moon. **In this run it lands fine** (`altM 1.62`, grounded, drift 0), and that is a measurement of one route rather than a guarantee. **This lane does not promise that a save taken on the moon returns correctly**, and the field belongs to physics (persistence R-BODY-1). What the trip does guarantee, and what `worldjump.mjs` asserts, is that the world you left comes back identical in its dig counts.
- **PROVENANCE, RECORDED BECAUSE AN UNATTRIBUTED WORKING TREE IS A HAZARD AND NOT A GIFT (GP-501, 2026-08-03).** Every file above was **already in the working tree, uncommitted and undocumented**, written at 12:23 to 12:37 today by a gameplay pass that ended without landing or logging anything. HEAD moved twice afterwards without it. It carried decision numbers **GP-450, GP-452 and GP-455, which the ledger has never allocated to anybody**, so this pass **renumbered them into its own allocated block** (GP-450 to GP-500, GP-452 to GP-502, GP-455 to GP-505; comments only, zero behaviour change, re-run green afterwards) and **burned the originals**. It was adopted rather than rewritten because it was verified rather than read: two independent green runs plus the negative control, on an isolated `git archive HEAD` tree at 892a868, with `npx tsc --noEmit` silent. **For Admin: a lane that ends mid-pass leaves work that is indistinguishable from another lane's in-flight edits**, and the only things that told them apart here were four hours of mtime and two intervening commits. That is luck of exactly the kind NUMBERS.md keeps naming.

- **SCOPED, NOT BUILT, AND THE BRIEF'S PREMISE IS WRONG IN THE PLAYER'S FAVOUR (GP-504, 2026-08-03) - THE AUTOPILOT IS NOT AVAILABLE BEFORE THE STATION, IT IS AVAILABLE TO NOBODY.** Admin's item 3 says `techs::FlightAutopilot` grants `parts::AutopilotModule = 0x010D` by research, so it arrives before the station and contradicts Reid's chain. The research half is real (`core/include/of/research.h:704`, `autopilot.unlockItems = {parts_items::AutopilotModule}`), but `research.h:701` also sets `autopilot.requiresMilestone = milestones::ReachedOrbit`, **and nothing in the game ever awards that milestone.** Verified directly rather than taken from the scout: `grep -rn "\.earn(" web/src/` returns **exactly one call site**, `web/src/game/PersistProgress.ts:39`, and it is inside **restore**. `MILESTONE.ReachedOrbit` is declared at `web/src/game/Research.ts:35` and referenced nowhere else. **So in a fresh survival world the autopilot tech is unreachable by any route except loading a save that already carried the milestone**, and `probes/research.js:265` asserts that state as correct ("the autopilot is STILL blocked", refusal matching `/orbit/i`). The move Reid wants is therefore **cheaper and less disruptive than it looked**: it is swapping a milestone that is never earned for one that is, not prising a part out of a working tech tree. **The right home is `milestones::DockedWithStation` appended at `research.h:199` alongside `ReachedOrbit` and `LandedOffWorld`**, because `SaveProgress.milestones` is already `number[]` and round-trips through `saveProgress`/`restoreProgress` with **no TypeScript type edit and no `SAVE_VERSION` bump**. **The cross-lane question is who fires it**, and it must not be invented twice: docking does not exist (`AutopilotRun.ts:505` records the ask to physics, `of_ap_arm_transfer` has no `terminal` argument, and `phase == Done` is the strongest claim a client can make), so the only reachable event today is the transient `PlayerBody.onDeck`, which is published per frame and never latched. **A physics lane is live on the hand-flown mission and owns where arrival is recorded; this lane consumes the flag and will not mint a second one.**
- **SCOPED, NOT BUILT, AND IT COLLIDES WITH A /core INVARIANT THAT IS STATED FOUR TIMES (GP-505, 2026-08-03) - REID'S PICKAXE GATE IS REFUSED BY `gameplay.h` TODAY, AND THE LOOSE STONES ARE EXACTLY WHAT MAKES IT LEGAL.** `core/include/of/gameplay.h` says at line 845 `"Tool helps but isn't required"`, at 1169 `Bare hands still always work (no bootstrap deadlock)`, at 1193 again, and at 1248 names it `the no-bootstrap-deadlock invariant`. `harvestNode` has **no early return on `!hasTool`**: the tool changes `pull` from `baseYield` to `toolYield` and nothing else (`kBareHandSwings = 6` against `kToolSwings = 3`). **That invariant is correct and must not simply be deleted**, because with nothing else on the ground a pickaxe gate makes the game unwinnable from a fresh spawn: the pickaxe recipe is `{RawIron x 1, Wood x 1}` (`gameplay.h:1288`) and raw iron comes out of a node. **Reid's two asks are one design and only work together**: loose stones are the bootstrap that makes the gate safe, so the invariant is not broken, it is **relocated from "every node yields to a bare hand" to "the ground yields to a bare hand"**. What exists already: `Stone = 0x0031` and `CrudePickaxe = 0x0039` are both real items, and `NODE_KIND.Rock` already yields Stone, so **no new item id is needed for the stone itself**. What does not exist: **any pickable-on-the-ground entity of any kind.** `web/src/world/Scatter.ts` props are pure decoration and structurally invisible to `NodeField.pick`; `web/src/game/RockField.ts` nodes are full harvest nodes. `web/src/assets/Registry.ts:322` already rules that *there are no inert rocks* above a derived 0.174 m cap, and records its own open tension (`R9`): **under the cap it cannot be seen, over it it lies to the crosshair.** A loose stone is precisely the third state that tension is asking for.
- **THE REFUSAL CHANNEL EXISTS AND THE GATE MUST NOT BE PUT WHERE THE PROBES CANNOT SEE IT (GP-505).** Today a failed harvest **says nothing at all**: `Interact.grant` (`web/src/player/Interact.ts:131`) increments `this.misses` and returns `false` on both failure paths with no HUD call, which is the "a node that does nothing when you swing at it is indistinguishable from a broken one" shape the brief names. The channel to speak on already exists and is `GameHud.flash` (`web/src/ui/GameHud.ts:145`), which is the refusal channel by its own doc comment; `GameHud.gain` is explicitly NOT for errors and `banner` is "a moment, not a message". The pattern to copy is `web/src/game/FactoryRefusal.ts`, where every refusal carries **both** a `reason` and a `fix` and neither is optional. **And the instrument trap, recorded before anybody builds it:** `__of.harvest(index)` reaches `Interact.harvestNow` (`Interact.ts:169`), which calls `grant()` directly and **deliberately skips reach and the swing**, so a tool gate placed in `Interact.step` rather than in `grant` or in `/core`'s `harvestNode` would be **invisible to every probe that uses that door**, and green. The gate belongs in `harvestNode` with the client speaking the refusal, which also keeps one authority.
- **WHAT THIS LANE NEEDS FROM WORLD-GEN, STATED RATHER THAN REACHED FOR (GP-505).** A world-gen lane is live on ruins, so this is published and not taken. The ask is **placement and persistence of a loose-stone scatter**, not its rules: a per-cell deterministic set on the same `(seed, latCell, lonCell, slot)` lattice `RockField.buildRock` already uses, so it saves as the existing `[latCell, lonCell, slot, remaining]` row shape (persistence 5.1) with no schema work; biome-weighted like `ROCK_DENSITY_KM2`; sitting **below** `DECOR_ROCK_MAX_H` in drawn height so it never competes with a harvest node in the crosshair; and **refused on Cinder by name** rather than seeded at zero, which is GP-287's own rule. Gameplay owns what a loose stone yields, that a bare hand takes it, and that a node without a pickaxe refuses.

## 8. Subagent delegation log
- **GP-850, 2026-08-14: `beltcargo.js`'s GP-762 corridor shortfall, DIAGNOSE-FIRST lane (`lane/beltcargo-corridor`), no subagents spawned.** GP-774/775 (`lane/probe-reds`) had already resolved GP-762's original "wrong rotation" diagnosis (the tail now mates on both rotations, `gapM: 3, align: 1, ok: true`) and left one honest red standing: `legRoom: 0` on both, the corridor scan's own reach topping out 2.00 cells past the tail against the 2.6 a leg needs, with a walk-closer attempt that hit what read as a genuine player-movement freeze and was recorded out of scope. This lane's job was to settle FIXTURE vs WORLD. **Settled: FIXTURE, and now fixed, `valid: true` on three consecutive runs.**

  **The measurement that answered it.** `FactoryGhost.march` (`web/src/game/FactoryGhost.ts:36`) caps the aim ray at `REACH_M = 9.0` m from the EYE; nothing moves the player between the tail search and the corridor scan, so a leg whose far cells sit past that combined reach is invisible to any yaw/pitch sweep, however wide, regardless of how much room the ground actually has. Rather than trust that reasoning alone, this lane added `axisProbe`: the same `aimGhost` adaptive hill-climb (10, 3.5, 1.2, 0.5 degree steps) the tail search already trusts, walked one grid step at a time straight down the leg, published per-cell (`aimed`, `ok`, `reason`, `missM`). Pre-walk it showed every cell out to six steps resolving to the SAME snapped point near the drill's own outlet socket (`missM` climbing 1.002, 2.004, 3.006... in lock-step with `k`, `reason: "snapped to #1 socket_item_out -> socket_belt_in"` on every one) — the hill-climb could not get past that snap, which is a different, more specific picture than "no room" and was the first sign this was instrumentation, not ground truth.

  **The walk, and why GP-775's freeze did not reproduce.** `demolish.js`'s own `walkToPoint` (GP-770/771) was copied in nearly verbatim rather than re-derived: short, re-aimed `KeyW` holds (5 to 60 frames, re-aiming between each), never GP-775's single 500-frame hold. With it, the axis probe changed completely: cells one through four past the tail resolved with sub-millimetre miss and an EMPTY reason (real open ground, not a snap), cells five and six still tapering off (`missM` 1.01, 2.01) as reach ran out further along — proof the ground has real room to at least four cells past the tail, well past the three a leg needs, and proof the walk itself works fine at this length; GP-775's freeze is not chased further here; the difference is documented and future lanes should note it moved on this file's own copy of the identical mechanism.

  **Three more bugs before the probe actually went green, none of them the world:**
  1. `legRoom` was still being read off the old fixed-degree grid scan (2 degrees yaw x 1.2 degrees pitch, ±18/-62 to -12 window), which is sized for the tail-search stand-off and, measured, still reported `legRoom: 0` even re-centred on the exact corridor the axis probe had just proven open — a fixed angular window covers less physical ground the closer the player stands. Fixed by reading `legRoom` off the axis probe directly (`k * CELL_M >= 2.6 && ok && missM < 0.5`); the grid scan is kept only to feed the cross-direction (corner) search, which still needs lateral coverage the single-axis probe does not give it.
  2. The drag's own opening look (`tailC.yaw`/`tailC.pitch`) is computed once, during the tail search, before any walk — and was left stale. Measured: with only fix (1) in place, the drag laid a geometrically perfect 6-tile L (`oneRun`, `hasInteriorCorner`, `hasStraights` all true) but `drillFeedsTail: false`, `links: []`, and the placed cells sat one row off the drill's own row — the classic shape of a stale aim landing one cell short once the eye has moved. Fixed by re-resolving the tail's angles from the post-walk position (`aimGhost(tailC.pos)` again) before the drag opens.
  3. The walk target was too shallow: standing back 3.2 m from a point 5 cells past the tail (net ~1.8 m past the tail) mated the straight leg but the drag landed 5 of the 6 tiles the setup gate needs, deterministically, on two separate runs. Deepened to stand back 2.5 m from a point 6 cells past the tail (net ~3.5 m past the tail); every downstream placement is still `aimGhost`'s own hill-climb, so nothing else needed to change. Drag now lays all 8 tiles every run.

  **Verified**: `beltcargo.js` green on 3 consecutive runs, identical `drag laid 8 tiles` / `setup: {oneRun:true, hasInteriorCorner:true, hasStraights:true, drillFeedsTail:true}` / `cornerMaxDevM: 0` each time. `assembler.js` run once, unedited: `valid: true`, reproducing the documented GP-761 stall flake exactly (`closedBySnapping: 14, tipShortM: 42.99`), confirming this lane's changes to `beltcargo.js` did not touch it. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npx vite build` clean, `node --check` clean on both probes. Files touched: `web/tools/smoke/probes/beltcargo.js` only. Full numbers in `docs/web/NUMBERS.md`'s GP-850 to GP-864 row.
- **GP-790 to GP-796, 2026-08-14: the KeyW "~500-frame player-movement freeze", DIAGNOSE-FIRST lane (`lane/keyw-freeze`), one read-only survey subagent.** The one subagent was worth it and its result changed the plan: it swept `docs/`, every controller file and the whole git log and established that **the entire written record of the symptom is one allocation row in `docs/web/NUMBERS.md:117`**, added by `73fbb50`, with no repro steps, no build sha, no mode and no frame rate. That is why this lane measured three fault shapes instead of one. Everything after that was measurement in this context, because the work was nine browser passes against one served build and delegating any of them would have cost more context than it saved. **Outcome: NOT REPRODUCED on HEAD, cause not named, no fix landed** (GP-792), two instruments left behind (`probes/keywfreeze.js`, `probes/keywmute.js`), one mechanism refuted with numbers (the floating-origin rebase, GP-793), one real unrelated defect found and routed rather than taken (`uiHeld` has no reference count, GP-795), and one harness trap that produced a perfect false reproduction of the reported symptom (GP-794).
- **GP-770 to GP-775, 2026-08-14: the demolish.js and persist.js inherited reds, the beltcargo.js GP-762 rotation choice, and the assembler.js GP-761 stone-haul stall, DIAGNOSE-FIRST lane (`lane/probe-reds`), no subagents spawned.** Four assigned reds, each needing its own mechanism, all pre-existing on `origin/main` at `8fb03eb`. **(a) `demolish.js`, "the smelter would not go down at the head", FIXED, GP-770/GP-771/GP-772, `valid: true`.** Three separate mechanisms stacked on one probe, none of them the game: **GP-770**, the smelter step reused `demolish.js`'s own belt-to-belt neighbour band (`NEAR`/`FAR`, 0.5 to 1.25 m, sized for two footprint-1 tiles) to judge a belt-to-SMELTER mate, which FS-73 moved to `stepsFor(belt, smelter)` = 3 cells (~3.006 m); a `machineBand(kind)` helper derives the true band from `of.game().factory.footprint` (the same table `assembler.js`'s GP-760 already reads for the identical reason) and the smelter goes down. **GP-771**, the drill "beyond the tail", took three measured attempts: a blind fixed-angle pitch sweep jumped straight over the target cell near the horizon (measured: -10.5 to -10.25 degrees skipped from "m1:1,3" to "m1:1,-3", five cells the WRONG way, in one step); aiming directly at the tail belt's own socket point (the fix that works for every OTHER kind, per `assembler.js`'s `tailOut`) measured `ok: true` nowhere in a 9x9-degree spiral, because `FactoryGhost.resolveGhost`'s own comment says a miner NEVER position-snaps, only its heading does; the actual fix aims the raw ground point at the true target cell (`stepsFor(belt, miner)` = 3 cells out) at a FIXED yaw (the scene's own measured flow axis) rather than a free yaw/pitch search, because a free search converged on the right position with the WRONG one of the site's four cardinal headings and produced a miner with zero links. A `walkToPoint` closes the distance first, the same fix every placement past `FactoryGhost.march`'s ~9 m reach needs post-FS-73. **GP-772**, the rebuild step assumed the player had not moved since laying the belts, true before GP-771's own walk; fixed to walk back to the removed tile's recorded 3D position and aim by a free yaw+pitch search rather than a stale remembered angle. **(b) `persist.js`, DW-17 red, FIXED, GP-773, `valid: true`.** Two mechanisms in one probe, both in the FIXTURE: **first**, `back.depleted` was read a flat 0.4 s after `of.load()`, and `ledger.treesPending`/`rocksPending` are `PersistLedger.ts`'s own documented deferral (a saved tree/rock depletion not yet rebuilt into `TreeField`'s `known` registry drains the moment the cell next materialises, never lost) — measured before the fix, `saved.depleted` carried 4 nodes, `back.depleted` carried 1, `ledger.treesPending` was 3, and a bounded poll (same shape as `demolish.js`'s residue wait) resolved it in one 0.5 s cycle every run. **Second**, the acceptance compared `ledger.nodesDepleted` (the CORE-only, index-keyed diff, which `Persist.ts` deliberately excludes outcrops/rocks/trees from) directly against `saved.depleted`'s full count (every kind); this probe's own harvest hits kind-0/1 nodes, which are world trees on this site, so the comparison could never pass once a run actually depleted one. Fixed to sum every category the ledger names (`nodesDepleted + trees + rocks + treesPending + rocksPending`) against the same total. **(c) `beltcargo.js`, "no straight leg of three cells along the outlet" (GP-762), PARTIALLY FIXED, GP-774/GP-775, still red, and said so.** The room-scan heuristic GP-762 named (a coarse dry sweep picking a rotation by counting nearby placeable cells) is REPLACED, not patched: `attemptDrill(rot)` now places the drill for REAL at rotation 0, runs the whole tail/leg search against what actually went down, and only tears it up and tries rotation 2 if that measures no room — the same "verify by placing" shape GP-770 to GP-772 used, rather than trusting a proxy. Two real defects surfaced and were fixed doing this: **GP-774**, the tail acceptance test checked `gapM`/`align` but never `t.ok`, and measured accepting a geometrically perfect (`gapM: 1, align: 1`) but UNPLACEABLE (`ok: false`) cell at rotation 0, after which the drag laid zero tiles; `t.ok` is now part of the test. **GP-775**, the SAME footprint-vs-mating-distance class as GP-770/771 one level up: the tail-search's `k` steps and gap band were still `[1, 2]` grid steps (pre-FS-73, when `stepsFor(belt, miner)` was 1), and a real 3-cell candidate (post-FS-73) measured outside that band; both are now derived from `FOOTPRINT`. **With both fixed, the tail mates correctly on BOTH rotations** (`gapM: 3, align: 1, ok: true`) — proving GP-762's original "wrong rotation" diagnosis is resolved — **but `legRoom: 0` on both**, because the corridor scan's own reach (unchanged since before FS-73) still measures from the player's original stand-point, and the tail sitting one cell further from the drill than it used to pushes the leg's own far cells past it (measured: scan tops out 2.00 cells past the tail, short of the 2.6 a leg needs by 0.4 m, on both rotations). **Walking the player closer first was attempted and hit a genuine, reproducible, unexplained movement freeze**: `KeyW` held for a full 9 s (500 frames) from the exact stand-point moved the player 0.000 m (feet bit-identical before and after) while `grounded: true`, `blockedByBuild: false`, `blockedByRock: false` and `slopeCos: 1` all read as an ordinary clear stand; `KeyS`/`KeyA`/`KeyD` each moved the eye a suspiciously IDENTICAL 9.053 m, which does not read as three different directions of ordinary strafing. This is a player-movement anomaly distinct from anything `FactoryGhost`/`FactorySnap`/`FactoryWiring` owns, out of this lane's scope to chase into the movement/physics code, and is recorded rather than forced past with an unverifiable workaround. **(d) `assembler.js`, GP-761's "stone haul stops ~43 m short of port B", INSTRUMENTED, PROVEN NON-DETERMINISTIC, NOT EDITED.** Two back-to-back runs of the identical, unmodified probe, same fixed world seed (`0xbf00d01`), same measured 43.2 m patch separation: the FIRST run's held drag (`hold: 5000` frames) closed the whole haul, `closedBySnapping: 4, tipShortM: -0.01` (past the port); the SECOND, immediately following, stalled at `closedBySnapping: 14` (budget exhausted), `tipShortM: 42.99` — the exact ~43 m shortfall the prior `lane/assembler-red` documented, from the SAME seed and SAME code. Same scenario, same code, two different outcomes: the stall is a timing/performance-sensitive property of how much real ground a fixed-frame-count held drag covers, not a reproducible-from-state code defect, so no code change was made (raising the closing budget was already tried by the prior lane and cost unbounded minutes without reliably finishing). **A separate, valuable finding from the run that succeeded: `assembler.js`'s own long-"expected-RED" `stoneArrivesByBelt` is ALREADY FIXED in `/core`** (FS-67's `Automation::inferItem`, `core/include/of/automation.h` line ~747, with `test_container.cpp`'s `assembler_second_ingredient_arrives_on_its_own_belt` as a standing regression test) — the successful run measured `stoneArrivesByBelt: true` and `portsMated: true` the moment the haul actually reached port B, which the prior lane's "recorded, not fixed" note never observed because that lane's own run was blocked earlier (on the smelter-outlet defect it WAS fixing) and its note inherited the probe's stale header prose rather than a fresh reading. Whenever the haul completes, the probe's own downstream `starvedMakesNothing`/`exhaustionStops` control-window assertions now fail, because they assume a genuinely stone-starved assembler that FS-67's fix means no longer exists on a belted line — a real, distinct fixture staleness recorded here rather than rewritten (roughly 190 lines of interlocking measurement windows), given the effort already spent on the other three items. **Verified across all four**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npx vite build` clean, `check:roles` PASS; `probes/factoryshot.js` and `probes/destruction.js` unregressed. Files touched: `web/tools/smoke/probes/demolish.js`, `persist.js`, `beltcargo.js` only (`assembler.js` read and instrumented, not edited). Full numbers in `docs/web/NUMBERS.md`'s GP-770 to GP-789 row and `docs/controllers/factory-sim.md` §8.
- **GP-760 to GP-762, 2026-08-14: `assembler.js`'s "no belt off the smelter outlet" red, DIAGNOSE-FIRST lane (`lane/assembler-red`), no subagents spawned.** Same class as GP-690's `enemies.js` reds: `tailOut`, the probe's own helper for aiming at a placed part's outlet, was `add(b.pos, b.fwd, b.kind === 'belt' ? 0.5 : 1.0)`, correct when the smelter and the drill were 2 m and wrong every time since FS-73 took both to 4 m (the same day as the `enemies.js` fixture, one commit later). Confirmed with numbers rather than assumed: the port GAP this probe's own runs report (`#1 miner socket_item_out -> socket_belt_in (0.50 m)`, `#3 belt socket_belt_out -> socket_item_in (0.50 m)`) at a measured 3-cell (3.006 m) mating distance both solve to a real outlet 2.006 m out, matching `footprint.smelter / 2` and `footprint.miner / 2` (4 / 2 = 2), not the literal 1.0 the probe carried. **GP-760: fixed** — `tailOut` now reads `of.game().factory.footprint` (the same table `machineports.js`/`autoline.js`/`shortline.js` already read for the identical reason) instead of a hardcoded literal. That alone was not the whole defect: `standBack`, the point the player walks to before aiming, was a flat `-3.2 m` from the (now-corrected) outlet along the run's own axis, which for a 2 m machine cleared its back face by 0.6 m and for a 4 m smelter landed 1.2 m BEHIND ITS OWN CENTRE — inside the housing, on the input face a short chain's own belts and drill already occupy. Measured directly: `walkTo`'s 30-iteration budget logged the identical eye position on every single step, wedged against it, and a 29x29-degree ghost sweep centred there found not one `ok:true, snapped` candidate in the whole cone, because the true output cell (three cells clear of the housing, `MachinePlacement.footprintsOverlap`'s own reach) was never inside it. Standing PAST the outlet instead (continuing the chain's own direction of travel) measured no better — a longer walk to a point further beyond where the player already stood, same wedge. Fixed the way GP-690's `placeUntil` fixed the same class of problem: WIDEN BY TRYING rather than compute one more single offset the next rescale falsifies again — `standCandidatesFor` tries several geometrically distinct stand-offs (past the outlet, and to either side along the chain's own measured right axis `R`) in turn, first one that lands a `snapPlace` wins; a belt tip keeps its original single always-working candidate. **Verified**, `probes/assembler.js`, sandbox: `valid: true`. `fails`: two remain, both recorded rather than chased further given the effort already spent on the assigned red. **`stoneArrivesByBelt`** is the probe's OWN documented, pre-existing, already-diagnosed defect (its header traces it to `Automation::inferItem` binding an inserter's item type at connect time against an empty belt, an `/core`/wiring fix, explicitly out of this lane's scope, and explicitly expected red by the probe's own comment). **`portsMated`** (port B, the stone inlet, never mates — `inletB: null`) is NEW information: once the smelter-outlet block cleared for the first time since FS-73, the stone haul's long second leg (tens of metres "down the port-B axis") measured stopping roughly 43 m short of the target even after the closing loop's original 14-press mop-up budget was exhausted. Raising that budget was tried (60 presses) and cost several unbounded minutes per run without reliably finishing inside this lane's time — not a trade this lane could responsibly validate — so the loop is left at its original 14 and the defect is **recorded as GP-761** (the stone-haul drag route itself likely needs FS-73-scale rework, not just a bigger mop-up budget) for factory-sim/gameplay to pick up. **GP-762, recorded, not fixed:** `beltcargo.js`'s pre-existing "no straight leg of three cells along the outlet" red is the SAME mechanism class one level up — not a stale offset but a stale ROTATION CHOICE. Its drill lands on the identical default site (`m1:1,2`) `assembler.js`'s iron drill uses, and the coarse pre-placement room scan (measured: 16 cells "ahead", 49 "behind") picks the outlet direction with more apparent room and orients the drill toward it — except `assembler.js`'s own real, exhaustive, 76-belt-long successful chain proves definitively that the OPPOSITE direction from this exact site is the one with abundant clear ground, while the chosen ("behind", 49) direction measures `legRoom: 0` after real placement, with only 2 cells free on the other side by the file's own diagnostic fallback (`roomBehindTheOutlet`). The file's own comment already names the mechanism ("the drill's outlet facing down the axis that has no ground to lay on, which is the rotation choice above getting it wrong") but has no retry; a fix belongs alongside GP-761 and needs the same care GP-760 took rather than a rushed edit under this lane's remaining time. **Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, `vite build` clean, `check:roles` PASS; `probes/factoryshot.js` unregressed (`valid: true`, unchanged by this lane's edits). Files touched: `web/tools/smoke/probes/assembler.js` only.
- **GP-690 to GP-692, 2026-08-13: the three `enemies.js` reds, DIAGNOSE-FIRST lane (`lane/enemy-reds`), no subagents spawned.** Delegation would have cost more context than it saved: all three reds live in one 460-line probe and each one needed the same instrumented run to settle. Method was the one NUMBERS.md asks for, an instrumented measurement before any edit: a throwaway probe that asserted nothing and only recorded the build ghost's verdict on every press, the whole live creature list diffed across every round, and the pool/live/nests/claimed quadruple side by side. **All three reds were the fixture measuring an older game and none was a game defect**, which is the same ratio §3 of NUMBERS.md records. (a) **Placement.** 10 presses, 3 machines, 7 refusals, every one of them `too close to #N smelter` with a named reason. Successes at ring cells (1,2), (5,5), (9,2); `footprintsOverlap` refuses inside a 4-cell box and `FOOTPRINT.smelter` is 4, so a 21-degree step around a 3.7 m ring cannot clear it. The probe was written 2026-07-27 (fb0723b) when a smelter was 2.00 m; FS-73 doubled it on 2026-07-28 (aea0d2c). Fixed by replacing the fixed press count with a `placeUntil` sweep (62-degree step, rings at pitch -18/-24/-13/-30, belts on their own ring because a 1-cell belt within two cells of a 4 m housing stands half inside it), and the count assertion went from `>= 5` to `=== 6` plus a new one that every refusal carried a sentence. Measured after: `{"smelter":6,"belt":3} in 6+4 presses, 1 refused`. (b) **Kills.** The detail string `0 -> 11 over 14 aimed rounds` was read as zero kills; the 0 is `k0.killed`, the counter before the volley. Traced round by round: 14 rounds, 14 hits, 0 ground hits, 11 bodies dead. The three non-fatal rounds are one body, a 75 hp Ravager sitting 0.25 m off the aim line at 4.12 m, which is 0.02 m NEARER along the ray than the 15 hp Skitterer at 4.14 m the loop aimed at, so `Weapon.fire` (nearest sphere the ray enters) put rounds 5, 6 and 7 into it, 75 to 53 to 31 to 9, and round 8 finished it. `22 damage against 15 hp is one round, one Skitterer` was an assumption about the ROSTER at evolution 0.208. Replaced with four assertions over the live-list diff: every round lands in a body and none in the dirt, each round puts its 22 points into exactly one body, a survivor loses exactly 22, and the kill count equals the rounds that emptied a body. The old line could not tell an overkilled Skitterer from a wounded Ravager because it never looked at a body. (c) **Pool.** RN-123 (c96af0a, 2026-07-29, two days after the probe was written) promotes the nearest `MAX_RIGS` creatures into skinned rigs which release their batch slot. Measured at the end of a fight: instances 36, live 40, nests 4, claimed 8. Nothing was double counted and the pool never lied; the identity gained a term. Both pool checks are now the equality `instances === live + standing nests - claimed` rather than the old `>=`, which would have been just as green with the batch leaking a slot per corpse. Also `nests` to `standing nests`, because `enemies.nests` counts dead ones and only a standing nest is drawn. **No game code changed.** `enemies.js` `fails: []` with `--combat=1` and with the no-combat control; `garrison.js --combat=1` `fails: []`.
- **GP-546 to GP-549, 2026-08-13: ruin interior interact + milestone (L7), IMPLEMENTATION lane, no subagents spawned.** Recorded because the choice not to delegate is itself a decision: the pick/prompt mechanism was MECHANICAL copying of `Antennas.pick`'s already-reviewed centre-and-radius shape, and the milestone-wiring/scope questions (gate Electrification, scope the antenna-upgrade tech out) were JUDGEMENTS this controller owns. **What was consumed, unchanged:** `research.h`'s milestone mechanism (`ReachedOrbit`'s own shape) and the `grantMilestone` entry point (GP-530); `Sites.visited`/`markVisited` (WG-151, poi.h); `RuinSites.ts`'s report and its new one-line `investigateLocal()` geometry accessor (world-gen's file, touched minimally per its own charter). **New**: `web/src/game/RuinInteract.ts` (pick, press, prompt); `Gameplay.aimedInvestigate`; a `pickAim` cascade entry and a `doInteract` branch. **Verified**: `core/tests` 42/42 suites green (`test_research_survival.cpp` D1–D4 and D7 updated for the new gate, D8 added); `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean; `check:limits` clean on every file this lane touched (`RuinSites.ts` trimmed back under 400 after crossing it by one line). `probes/ruininvest.js`, sandbox, 640x360: `valid: true, pass: true, fails: []` — out-of-range does nothing, the walked doorway crossing and the socket press grant the milestone exactly once (three independent readings: the per-ruin `visited` bit, the milestone list, and a `console.info` watch on `grantMilestone`'s own documented log line), Electrification's `block` moves off `MilestoneMissing` the instant the grant lands, a second press is a named, logged-nothing refusal, and a save+reload keeps both bits while logging ZERO grants during the load itself. `probes/ruinplace.js` re-run on the same build: `valid: true, fails: []`, no regression. Two harness defects found IN THE PROBE and fixed rather than shipped: `aimAtPoint`'s inherited ±96° capture range (widened to a guaranteed 360° first pass, `ruinplace.js` itself untouched) and a mistaken assumption that the investigate socket is standable ground rather than the centre of a solid object picked at range like a machine (confirmed with `of.solidBuild`, the assertion corrected rather than deleted).
