- **GP-533 to GP-539, 2026-08-13: the scanning antenna (L6, the reveal lane consuming L5's substrates), IMPLEMENTATION lane, no subagents spawned.** Recorded because the choice not to delegate is itself a decision: every question was either a JUDGEMENT this controller owns (the price, the tech's prereq) or MECHANICAL copying of an already-reviewed pattern (`ResearchStations.ts` -> `Antennas.ts`, `LaunchPadSave.ts` -> `AntennaSave.ts`), and briefing a subagent on either would have cost more than doing it. **What was consumed, unchanged:** GP-520 to GP-523's `MarkerRegistry`/`MapMarker` (the real producer this lane was always going to be, landing against the SAME `MarkerRegistry.add` the debug stand-in used); WG-151's `of_poi_near`/`of_poi_mark_known` (ABI 24, no bump needed — confirmed `abi=24` live rather than assumed); D-019's whole `StructureDef`/`ResearchStations.ts` pattern. **Two things this lane found and fixed in itself rather than shipping**: `Hotbar.ts` and `Persist.ts` both crossed `check:limits`' 400-line cap by a handful of lines (399 -> 405 and 391 -> 437), caught by re-running the gate rather than assumed clean because `Gameplay.ts` and `GameplayActions.ts` were already over it before this lane touched them; both trimmed back under, `Persist.ts` by extracting `AntennaSave.ts` (`LaunchPadSave.ts`'s own precedent, which also slimmed the antenna's save/restore logic out of an already-dense file). And the probe's own first run under-budgeted its harvest by conflating science SPENT with science MADE (see the NUMBERS.md row) — found by the probe going red with a real, readable cause rather than by inspection, fixed, green on the immediate re-run. **Verified**, `probes/antenna.js`, survival, 640x360: `valid: true, pass: true, fails: []`; `of.sites().stats.known` 0 -> 1; one `ruin` marker, `known: true`, unit `dirBody`; `of.goals().satisfied` finds `antenna` flipping `false -> true`; the bill `25 Iron + 20 Copper + 15 Stone` billed exactly (`bill "..." -> spent {"Stone":15,"Iron":25,"Copper":20}`); and a same-run idempotence check (a second `siteMarkKnown` on the already-known site returns `false`, marker count unchanged). **The negative control ran FIRST, not last**: before any station or antenna existed, `of.sites().stats.known === 0` and `of.markers().count === 0` were asserted, so "the antenna revealed it" cannot be equally true of a world that reveals everything by default. `core/tests`: `research_station_tests` (now carrying `test_scanning_antenna.cpp` alongside `test_research_station.cpp`, the identical seven-part shape) plus the two pre-existing suites (`survival_slice_tests`, `research_tests`) whose own pinned counts (`structureDefs().size()`, `allItems().size()`, `survivalTechs().size()`) this lane's append moved — all 42 ctest suites green, baseline held. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean, `check:roles`/`check:proxies` untouched and green. **Owed, named rather than attempted under time pressure**: GP-539's `SiteCatalog::insideAnySite` wiring, and `structures/scanning_antenna.glb` from the art lane against the placeholder `power_pole.glb` this lane borrowed (stated in `Antennas.ts`'s header, `IconTable.ts`, `gameplay.h`'s TypeId comment and the live `placeholderMesh` report field, D-019's own four-places rule). **The second of those was DISCHARGED 2026-08-14 by A6; see GP-619's own note.**
- **GP-650 to GP-654, 2026-08-11: the map's body. Reid's player report on build cfeffad, DIAGNOSE+FIX worker lane, no further delegation.** Two symptoms, one shared cause and one separate one. **The report:** on the moon, the map draws the space station in orbit around Cinder and the panel reads Anchorage AP/PE 800.0/800.0 km; back on Forge the same row reads 400.0/400.0. **Measured first, in one driven run:** the conic never moved (`a = 1000000.0000000008 m`, `e = 5.3e-16`, read off `of.flight('vessels')` on both bodies), so 1,000,000 minus 600,000 and 1,000,000 minus 200,000 are the two figures and the altitude was simply being taken against whichever body the player stood on. **(A) The AP/PE and the drawn orbit.** `SaveWorlds.ts` classifies `vessels` as a GLOBAL save key, deliberately and correctly, so every record crosses to the moon with the player — but `VesselRecord` carried no body, so `MapMode.vesselRows` subtracted `d.bodyRadiusM` (the observer's), `Map3D.syncMarkers` and `OrbitLines.syncRails` placed every record in the observer's frame with no test, and `AutopilotTargets.registrySource` did the same arithmetic in the planner's own list. Fixed with `VesselRecord.bodyId` (/core's `BodyParams::bodyId`), stamped at every mint site (`mintStation` via `installStation`/`StationInstallDeps`, `FlightVessels.makeRecord` off `oracle.body.bodyId`) and RECOVERED for a legacy row in `adoptSaved` from its conic's own `mu`, which is the number `of_orb_park` was handed and therefore not a guess. `SAVE_VERSION` does not move, under the rule `vessels`/`player`/`dayT` were added by. **One reader, `web/src/world/VesselBody.ts`**, backed by `readFacts` (the client's existing single reader of `of_body_facts`, RN-845) so a body nobody is standing on can still be named: **no fifth bodyId-to-word table**, which is the trap `SaveSlots.ts` counted four instances of and refused to add to. **(B) The map's own body was a boot-time copy.** `bootMap` captured `radiusM`, `muM3S2` and the atmosphere ceiling once and `MapFocus` carried the string literal `'Forge'`; `WorldSession.reboot` swaps the body and rebuilds none of it. **Measured at HEAD through `of.reboot(1)`: `globeRadiusUnits` 5.937 before and 5.937 after, and `focus.options` still `["you","Forge"]` while standing on Cinder.** Replaced by ONE thunk onto `SurfaceOracle.body` — the client's live-body authority by its own documentation, and the one `WorldSession.reboot` already RE-SEATS — so the map follows the world with no subscription and no rebuild. `Map3D` now borrows `PlanetProxy`'s geometry while the proxy IS the live body (the shipped path, unchanged, zero extra VRAM, biome tint intact) and builds its own neutral sphere at `proxyRadiusUnits(liveBody)` when it is not, reporting `globeSource` so the two are separable. **Verified:** `tools/smoke/probes/mapbody.js`, `valid: true`, `fails: []` — Forge 400 km derived from the record rather than typed; Cinder globe 5.937 -> 1.9475 units with the ratio checked against the two bodies' own radii; zero rails lines, zero vessel markers and `skippedElsewhere >= 1` on Cinder; the panel row kept and NAMING Forge with its AP unchanged at 400 km; a computed negative control proving the old arithmetic would have printed 800,000 m there, 400 km away from what the panel shows; and the whole thing reverting exactly (`globeRadiusUnits` back to 5.937 within 1e-9, `globeSource` back to `proxy`) on the way home. **TWO PRE-EXISTING RED PROBES FOUND AND ATTRIBUTED, NOT INHERITED SILENTLY.** `map3d.js` fails on this branch and **fails identically on unmodified `f0a7da0`**, proven by building the parent commit into a second worktree, serving both, and running one small probe that isolates exactly the two failing claims (no VAB build, no orbital flight, minutes instead of 45): **(1) section 4 "THE SEAT MOVED" is a probe-fixture defect that landed when Anchorage started shipping in the registry at boot.** `map3d.js` targets `rows.find((v) => v.mode === 'rails')`, and the FIRST rails record is now always the station (id 1, present before the probe builds anything), not the rocket it just flew to orbit and left. Taking control of the station -- an `emptyDesign()` record -- cannot seat, so every assertion below it fails. Measured on BOTH builds, byte for byte the same: `aboard false`, `promotedId 0`, `altM 1.6200000001117587` unchanged, `status CLAMPED`. The fix is one line in the probe (`find` the rails record whose id is the one it rolled out) and it belongs to whoever owns `map3d.js`, not to this lane, which would otherwise be editing a probe to make its own work look green. **(2) "CLOSED COST IS ZERO" is measuring an unsettled scene.** The pre-open draw-call count is **not stable run to run on one build**: two runs of the SAME branch build read 73 and 74. The probe's own comment already warns that streaming converges at spawn and a mid-stream read is a different scene; the invariant needs a converged read, which `worldreboot.js`'s `settle()` already knows how to take. Both runs end 74 -> 72 or 73 -> 72, on branch and on HEAD alike. **`markers.js` is viewport-sensitive and its header does not say so**: it is green at the default 1600x900 (`valid: true`, `fails: []`, both tags in the 2D draw report) and red at `--width=320 --height=200`, because the flat map canvas has no room left for the marker layer. A note on its invocation line would save the next lane the hour this cost. **Green on this branch:** `mapbody.js`, `stationboard.js` (`valid: true`, `fails: []`), `check:pose` (14 checks), `tsc --noEmit`, `vite build`. `check:limits` exits 1 on this branch and exits 1 on `f0a7da0` too, on 20 files this lane did not create. **Named limits:** the player's own door is a page RELOAD (`VisitWorlds.ts`), so symptom (B) is reproduced and proven through `of.reboot`, the only in-page switch this client has and the harsher case; on the reload path the proxy is rebuilt for the arriving body, and what remains there is that the map's globe is an untinted grey Lambert in survival, so Forge and Cinder are told apart by size and relief alone — the focus option now names the body, which is the smallest honest fix for "which world am I looking at" and is not a substitute for tinting it. The planner still has no inter-body transfer, so a target at another body is a BLOCKED row naming it rather than a plannable one.
- **GP-520 to GP-532, 2026-08-11: the scanning-spine L5 substrates (map marker registry + milestone bus), IMPLEMENTATION worker lane, no further delegation.** Two independent things, each landed against real signals rather than trusted by inspection. **(A) Markers (GP-520 to GP-523).** One shared type `MapMarker` (`web/src/ui/MapTypes.ts`: `{key, kind: 'ruin'|'signal'|'deposit', dirBody, label, known}`) and one authority, `MarkerRegistry` (`web/src/game/MarkerRegistry.ts`, module-level singleton `markerRegistry`, keyed exactly like `VesselRegistry` so a rescan overwrites rather than duplicates). Both maps read it and neither computes a marker's world position independently: `MapPaint.markerPosM(dirBody, bodyRadiusM)` is the ONE conversion, called from `MapLayers.drawMarkers` (2D) and `Map3D.syncMarkers` (3D). **`known` is the only gate**, deliberately not the terrain layer's `reveal || seen` test (`MapLayers.ts` comment says so at the gate itself) — a scan revealing a marker on unwalked ground is the entire point of a scan. Debug source: `of.markers('add'|'remove'|'clear'|'list', ...)` (`web/src/app/DebugMarkers.ts`), the stand-in until L6 (the reveal lane) becomes the real producer against the SAME `MarkerRegistry.add`. **Verified**, `tools/smoke/probes/markers.js`: a marker's drawn pixel matches an independently-computed `toPx(markerPosM(dirBody, bodyRadiusM))` to 0 px error; `known: false` is absent from the draw report; a known marker 6 km from spawn (past the local survey horizon) still draws while `surveyFraction` reads 0.00001 and `revealAll` is false; the 3D census counts both kinds. First run caught its own miscalibration (an offset placed 314 km out, past every span the fixture actually zoomed to) — recorded as the finding it is, not smoothed over. **(B) Milestones (GP-530 to GP-532).** `grantMilestone(r, m, cause)` (`web/src/game/Research.ts`) wraps `Research.earn` with a logged cause; idempotence is `research.h`'s `setMilestone`, unchanged, not re-implemented. Wired in `web/src/app/Systems.ts` (the same file that already crosses flight and the map/board/recover keys): a RISING edge on `FlightSession.status` grants `ReachedOrbit` on `'ORBIT'` and `LandedOffWorld` on `'DOWN'` when `s.body.bodyId !== 0`, gated on `f.aboard`. **The restore path is untouched** — `PersistProgress.ts:39` still calls `research.earn` directly, never `grantMilestone`, exactly as the brief demanded. **Verified at the ctest level, not the browser**, and that split is a finding worth stating plainly. `core/tests/test_research.cpp` gained `milestone_grant_is_idempotent_and_gates_the_tech` and `milestone_restore_of_an_already_held_id_is_a_no_op` (both green: not earned yet, earned once, a second grant is a no-op, a fresh state restoring an already-held id is also a no-op); discovered ALONGSIDE them that `core/tests/test_research_survival.cpp`'s pre-existing `survival_autopilot_needs_the_orbit_flown_by_hand` **already proves the full end-to-end claim this brief asked for probe-level** — `FlightAutopilot.canResearch` flips true and `tryResearch` succeeds the instant `ReachedOrbit` is granted, with `Electrification` and full science stocked — and was green before this lane touched anything. `research_tests`: 17 tests, 288 checks, all green. **The LIVE browser wiring (does a real flight's ORBIT transition actually fire `grantMilestone` in the running client) is UNVERIFIED tonight**, and named as a refusal rather than papered over: `tools/smoke/probes/milestones.js` was written to drive it exactly the way `map3d.js`'s own fixture reaches orbit (build, roll out, board, the pause-menu "Teleport to orbit" cheat), but the shared 16-core VM was running at a load average of 33 to 38 for this entire session (several sibling lanes' own Chrome instances measured concurrently at 350 to 630% CPU each, `uptime`/`ps` both checked to confirm this rather than assumed) — a single build-and-orbit cycle that `map3d.js` completes in about 29 minutes on a quiet VM did not finish in over 90 minutes on two attempts, the second already trimmed to one vessel instead of two once the ctest above made the second cycle's idempotence claim redundant. Both attempts were confirmed still actively computing (500%+ CPU on their own gpu-process, not stalled) before being killed for time; neither ever produced a result. `LandedOffWorld` is wired identically and is ALSO not live-exercised: there is no land/deorbit cheat and Cinder is airless, so proving it live needs a real multi-burn transfer, independently out of scope for one night regardless of VM load. `TypeScript typecheck` and `vite build` are clean with the wiring in place, which is the compile-time evidence available; the runtime claim is open. A new minimal read-only debug op, `of.research()` (`web/src/app/DebugGameplay.ts`, `Research.list()`), was added for this probe and is unused until it runs. **What L6 consumes:** the `MarkerRegistry`/`MapMarker` API above, unchanged and fully verified; `grantMilestone` is available for any future live-play grant site, with its own mechanism (not its live wiring) verified.
- **GP-506, 2026-08-11: LOOSE STONES + THE PICKAXE GATE, STORYLINE RUNG 2** (implementation lane, `lane/stones-pickaxe`). Reid's order (gather wood bare, gather loose stone bare, craft a stone pickaxe, only then mine coal/iron/copper) is now real: `worldgen::survival::requiresToolFor(NodeKind)` is a new DATA gate (`gameplay.h`) read by `harvestNode`/`harvestPatch` — false for Tree/Rock (bare hands always work), true for CoalSeam/IronOre/CopperOre. A refused swing returns a named `HarvestRefusal::ToolRequired` (never a silent zero granted, never a mutation to the node), published across the wasm bridge as ABI 23 (`of_gp_node_harvest`'s scratch grows a 5th `refusal` word; new pure query `of_gp_node_harvest_gate` lets the client ask before committing a swing's cooldown). **`recipeCrudePickaxe`/`recipeCrudeAxe` changed from RawIron+Wood to Stone x2 + Wood x1** — the deadlock breaker: the old bill needed ore, which the new gate makes unreachable bare-handed, so a fresh spawn could never craft the tool that unlocks ore. `PrimitiveFurnace` (5 Wood + 2 RawIron) is correctly left behind the pickaxe. Client-side: `Interact.ts`'s swing commit now asks `harvestGate` BEFORE paying the cooldown/animation, so a refusal (unlike an empty node or a full pack, which can only be found out by swinging) costs nothing; `GameCore.ts` gained `HARVEST_REFUSAL`/`harvestRefusalText` mirroring `CRAFT_BLOCK`'s shape, wired to a HUD flash. `ObjectiveList.ts` gained a `stone` card between `wood` and `tool` and reworded both `tool` (states the new bill) and `ore` (states the pickaxe prerequisite). **PREMISE CORRECTED, LOUDLY:** the brief's item 1 (a new world-gen lattice for loose stones, retiring render-only pebble decor) was **already shipped** — `RockField.ts`/`RockTuning.ts` (WG-67 to WG-72, commit `d734a87`, predates this brief) already place real bare-hand-harvestable `NodeKind::Rock` nodes globally by biome density, and already retired the lying decor pebbles above a derived `DECOR_ROCK_MAX_H`; nothing there was touched. Also found: `SiteCatalog::insideAnySite` (poi.h:790) is C++-only and reaches neither TreeField nor RockField (TreeField uses its own hardcoded spawn-clearing snapshot instead) — a corrected premise for whichever domain eventually wires POI keep-out into the web placement layer. **Tests:** `core/tests/test_survival_slice.cpp` — new gate/refusal cases, a full-pack-keeps-the-stone case, and a dedicated anti-deadlock bootstrap test that walks an empty inventory to a crafted pickaxe using only Tree/Rock nodes (asserts the recipe's ingredients are bare-hand-only, so it goes red if the bill is ever re-tightened toward ore); three pre-existing tests rewritten for the new recipe/legal order. 41/41 ctest suites green (baseline: 41). **Browser probes ran into a pre-existing VM-wide WebGL-context-loss issue under GPU contention (confirmed via `npm run check:boot` failing identically, unrelated to this change); the ABI 23 handshake itself verified clean in every attempt (wasm reports 23, client agrees). New probe `pickaxegate.js` and the rewritten `objectives.js` are written and ready but UNRUN — flagged for the verify lane to run once the VM is less contended.** Numbers: GP-506 used for the whole landing (one coherent commit); GP-507 onward free.
- **GP-600 to GP-607, 2026-08-03: TWO SUBAGENTS, ONE READ-ONLY SURVEY AND ONE CROSS-DOMAIN CONSULTATION.** (1) *Finish the qolbuild survey* (general-purpose; isolated tree, its own port, read-only, wrote nothing into the repo, torn down): ran all 13 committed stages of `qolbuild1-3.js` plus four scratch stages to reach the launch-pad refusal, the chest, the assembler recipe menu and the VAB, none of which any committed stage selects. Its most valuable output was **against its own instrument** (`innerText` falling back to `textContent` on an unrendered element, so hidden panels reported stale strings as drawn) and the observation that **none of the 13 stages contains a single assertion**. Folded into GP-606. (2) *Belt direction and idle cue* (factory-sim-controller; consultation only, built nothing): asked whose vocabulary the three missing cues belong to before inventing any. **Rulings received and accepted:** `Placed.fwd` in `web/src/game/FactoryKinds.ts` is the ONE authority for belt direction and the quaternion is derived FROM it, so nothing may re-derive a heading; `portsOf`/`portOf` in `web/src/game/FactoryPorts.ts` already publish each port's world position and outward normal, and **deriving a face from `FOOTPRINT` would have been the fifth copy of the constant FS-73 found in four places** (the assembler is 8 m of footprint and its sockets are nowhere near half of it); the scrolling belt-deck band is gated on item density, so **it vanishes on an empty or stalled belt**, which is exactly when direction is needed, and is flow feedback rather than a direction cue. **There is no idle REASON, only a four-value visual hint that fuses `starved` and `blocked` into one value**, and the client cannot separate them because recipe `inputCount` and machine `outCap` are not exported. factory-sim owns and will add the reason enum (their D-FS-97) on `research.h`'s shape: a code plus the offending id, client composes the sentence. **The drawing is mine, the value is theirs.** Blocked on their export; not started.

(2) *The autopilot behind the station*: where the part/tech gate lives, what the VAB actually checks, whether a "visited the station" record exists, the shape of `progress`, and whether docking is implemented.
